That search covered the station shown, the list, the Record tab and the head of the page, and left out the Primer tab: see the known limits of the lab under Faculty and curriculum.
- **An observation with a loop that was not clean** was told that it does not count and why, in the colour used for a reason and with no tick or cross, and neither the hints nor the expected observation came with it in Challenge.
- **Colour is not the only signal.** The colour of the flame and of each line and band is said in words when it is looked at. A line or a band not yet looked at has a dashed edge and is listed under the strip, in words, as not looked at. How bright a line or a band is beside the others is in its callout's words, so the brightest can be found without seeing the picture. A line is told from a band by its shape as well as by its callout: a thin stroke the strip's whole height, with dark either side of it, against a wider stretch that is shorter. A station's progress and each result on the Record tab are words, with a mark beside some of them. The solution's colour is said in words on the bottle's tile.
- **Reduced motion.** With the operating system set to reduce motion the flame carried no animation and nothing on the page animated. With motion allowed the flame flickers five times, for under five seconds, and then is still; automated tests hold the stylesheet to that.
- **Reload, Restart and other tabs.** A reload in the middle of a station came back on the Lab tab at that station, with the loop as it was, the flame and every look made, the strip out, the questions open, and what had been chosen and not checked gone. Restart asked first and then showed the first station of a new run. A second tab on the lab took a Restart made in the first. A stored record that does not fit is covered by an automated test of the page: it is set aside with the notice. At the review the page was reloaded with the loop dipped and not held, held and not looked at, looked at with answers chosen and not checked, with the warning showing, with an observation recorded that did not count, with the six stations done and the closing question open, with a wrong closing answer, and complete; each came back as the stored record has it, and what was chosen and not checked was gone. Four stored records that do not fit (not JSON, another version, a look at something the loop did not show, a status the entries do not give) were each set aside with the notice and kept under a backup key. With two tabs open, a warning showing in one went when the other saved, and an action in the first after that was added to what the second had done.
- **Sizes.** At 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768, 1366×768 and 1920×1080 the page did not scroll sideways, the strip was not scrolled inside its card, and every button and every choice of a station was at least 44 pixels each way. The strip was 447 pixels wide at the first of those sizes, 499 at the second, and 703 to 720 at the others. Each line's and band's button was at least 44 pixels wide and as tall as the strip at the stations tried with a clean loop; with a handled loop at the barium station, where three things are close together, the narrowest was 22 pixels or more wide. The controls drawn smaller than 44 pixels (the disclosures, the "Stations" button, Previous and Next, and the warning's two buttons) have the hit area of 44 pixels that the lab pages lay over a small control. The list of stations was behind its button at the first three sizes and beside the station at the last two.
- **The strip's lines and bands against its black.** Measured at the review from screenshots at 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768 and 1366×768, in both themes: every line of the six strips had a contrast of 3.0 to 1 or more with the black over two columns of pixels or more, with black either side of it, and every band 2.9 to 1 or more along it. The lab holds whatever it draws on the strip to 3 to 1: where the colour of a wavelength at the strength of its rank falls short, the colour is drawn paler. Before the review calcium's violet line stood at 1.5 to 1 and strontium's blue line at 2.2. Every line and band was drawn at its record's wavelengths, the numbers of the scale each under its own mark, and nothing outside 400 to 780 nm.
- **The ways in.** Home, the list of labs and the search dialog each showed the lab and opened it. In a window 600 pixels wide the Lab tab showed the notice in place of the stations, with no Start.
- **Both themes** were loaded, the dark card of the flame and of the strip was dark in both, and the browser's console was empty throughout.

**What has not been checked:**

- **No screen reader was used.** What is announced when the loop's state changes, when a part of a picture is looked at, when the warning appears, when feedback comes or when the entry shown changes has not been heard by anyone. The roles, names and states were read by script.
- **Chromium only, and no real device.** No other browser, no phone and no tablet. The taps were made by script in a desktop Chromium that emulates a touch screen at 820×1180; no finger has touched the page.
- **Contrast was measured on the strip's lines and bands and on nothing else:** not the coloured text, not the grey text, not the drawn flame, not the dashed edges of the buttons and not the scale.
- Browser zoom, enlarged text and forced-colour (high contrast) mode have not been tried. No person with a colour-vision deficiency has looked at the page.
- Nobody has run the lab by hand, with a keyboard or otherwise. The one reviewer that did not build the screens was another AI coding assistant, working by script.
- The review used four window sizes, 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768 and 1366×768, and no other.

**Known limits:**

- **The stations need a window at least 768 CSS pixels wide**, as the other labs' benches do; a narrower window has the primer and the record of a saved run.
- **A pointer that crosses a part has looked at it.** No time is asked of it, so a mouse swept along the strip opens the two questions that wait for it. Focus by keyboard looks in the same way.
- In a card narrower than 440 pixels the strip scrolls sideways, and a callout there can be cut by the card's edge. No window that shows a bench was found to leave the card that narrow.
- With several salts on a loop that was not clean, lines and bands can lie on top of one another; each still has a button of its own, at least 22 pixels wide, which then lies beside its line more than over it.
- What is chosen and not yet checked is lost on a reload, and so is whether the spectroscope was being looked through if no line or band had yet been looked at.
- **At the head of the page,** which the labs share: "Start the lab" is drawn 32 pixels tall, and the mode's line ("Mode: Guided") had a hit area 40 pixels tall where it is meant to have 44, the bar of tabs under it lying over its foot. Both were measured by tap at 820×1180 at the review and neither was changed.
- A window made narrower than 768 pixels and then wide again shows the stations afresh, as in the other labs: what was chosen and not checked is lost. A window 1366 pixels wide at a zoom of 200 per cent is narrower than that, and has no bench.
- The pictures are each one image to assistive technology, with a name and a line that says what to do with them; the whole strip is not described in one sentence on the page, because that sentence names the brightest thing, which is what two of the questions ask.
- Each station starts with its "Hazards" disclosure closed.

### The calorimetry lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built and checked on 2026-10-09 by the AI coding assistant that wrote them. Scripts drove the production build in headless Chromium 153 on Linux: 226 checks in five scripts, all passing at the last run. One whole lab was driven by the keyboard alone (Tab, Shift+Tab, Enter, Space, Home, End, the arrow keys, and the typing of the entries), one part and the graph by taps in a browser context that emulates a touch screen, and the rest with a mouse pointer. The review of the lab was held the same day by an AI coding assistant that had not built it, in the same way: scripts of its own drove the production build in the same browser, with 259 checks in five scripts passing at the review's last run, which was made after its last change to the code, and 264 when they were run again on 2026-10-10, after the one change made since. What it ran and measured is under "At the review of 2026-10-09" below, and what it found and changed is the last section of `docs/research/calorimetry/README.md`. Neither assistant's scripts are part of the repository. No person took part in either, and how the lab looks (colour, spacing, proportion, the drawn cup and the graph) is for the project's author to judge by eye and is not reported here.

**What was checked when the screens were built.** The list is of the page as it was first built; where the review changed what a point describes, the point says so.

- **A whole lab by keyboard.** One complete Guided lab at 1024×768 in the light theme, from "Start the lab". In part 1 the change was started before the lid and the stirrer, the warning was answered with "Go back", and the lid was put on and the stirrer started. In each part "Run on" was pressed until the notebook opened, the graph was entered at its one stop, every reading was looked at with the right arrow key, Home and End were tried, and the worksheet was typed in and its choice made with the arrow keys or Space; then on to the line that says the lab is complete, and to the Record tab. Three entries were first made wrongly, and each refusal said what it was and left focus in the field: a temperature the logger does not show, the heat of the water with the salt left out, and a result without its sign. The entries are typed; nothing is dragged, and nothing depends on timing or on a held key.
- **By tap.** At 820×1180, part 3 in Guided from an untouched cup to a run that counts: every reading looked at with the "Later reading" button, five entries typed and the metal chosen. At 768×1024, a cup worked, and readings looked at by taps on the graph and with the two buttons under it.
- **In Challenge, by mouse.** At 1024×768, part 2 with the lid left off: an entry not written as its field asks was refused, an entry was changed and the entries under it emptied, the worksheet was checked, the run was told that it does not count and why, the part was run again and counted, and the Record tab listed both runs. At 1366×768 in the dark theme, a whole lab, with part 1 run first without the stirrer.
- **Looking.** A mouse pointer over a reading's column, keyboard focus on it, or a tap rings the point and shows a callout that says its time and its temperature; the same words go to a status line for a screen reader, and the look is stored the first time. The callout stayed when the pointer left and when a field of the worksheet was tapped, and went at Escape. At the first and at the last reading it was inside the picture and over no point of the graph. The first entry stayed closed until a reading before the change had been looked at.
- **Focus.** Start moves focus to the Lab tab's panel. The warning takes focus when it is raised, and again when the same press raises it again. "Go back" puts focus on the step that was skipped, the lid before the stirrer; "Start the change anyway" puts it on the button that starts the change. The cup's buttons and "Run on" keep focus when pressed. An entry that is taken leaves focus on its button, which then reads "Change"; "Change" puts focus in the field with the old entry selected, and Escape or "Cancel" puts it back on the button. When the worksheet is checked, in Guided at its last entry, focus goes to the sentence that says how the run came out. "Run this part again", Previous, Next and the list of parts move focus to the heading of the part shown.
- **Tab order.** In part 1 with the cup worked and two entries made, the Tab key went from the heading through 20 stops, in the order they are read: the two bottles' hazards; the lid, the stirrer, the logger and the change; "Run on"; the graph, as one stop; the two buttons under it; the disclosure of the graph in words; the calculator; the "Change" of each of the two entries; the field that waits and its "Enter"; the disclosure of its relation; "Run this part again"; the sources; and Next.
- **Controls that cannot be used yet.** A button that cannot be used stays in the tab order and is marked unavailable; pressing it says why in its card's message line, which is a status region. Tried: a step of the cup already taken, "Check the worksheet" with entries missing, and "Run on" on a logger that is full. The entry that waits for a look at the graph is disabled, with what to look at first as text under its field, and that text is the description of its button.
- **Challenge gives nothing away before a run counts.** Automated tests search the Lab tab's markup, for each part, at every step of a careful run from an untouched cup to the last entry, for the figure the result is held to, the part's notes, the relation of an entry and the words that say whether an entry follows; and, after a check that does not count, for the same and for how far off the result was. None is there. In the browser, a change to Challenge in the middle of a worksheet took the relations and the word "Follows" off the page. As first built the sentence that says what a part finds was kept back too, because for parts 1 and 2 it said which way the heat goes; since the review it says neither and is shown in both modes, and the tests search instead for any word of which way the heat goes outside the two choices of the entry that asks it. The Primer tab is not part of that: see the known limits of the lab under Faculty and curriculum.
- **Colour is not the only signal.** The cup's state is said in words beside its drawing, and the drawing is hidden from a screen reader. A reading's time and temperature are said in words when it is looked at. A point that has been looked at is filled and one that has not is open, the key under the graph says so in words, and the name of each reading's button says whether it has been looked at. A step of the cup that has been taken carries a tick. A part's progress, whether an entry follows, how a run came out and each result on the Record tab are words, with a mark beside some of them. The graph and the cup are drawn in the colour of the page's text.
- **Reduced motion.** With the operating system set to reduce motion the stirrer carried no animation and nothing on the page animated. With motion allowed the stirrer bobs four times as the drawing first shows it going, and 3.6 seconds later it was still; automated tests hold the stylesheet to under five seconds. Marks about the stirrer's ring say that it is going in both cases.
- **Reload, Restart and other tabs.** A reload in the middle of a worksheet came back on the Lab tab at that part, with the cup, the graph, every look and the entries made as they were, with what had been typed and not entered gone, and with no callout. A reload with the warning showing came back without it and with the change not started. Restart asked first: refused, it changed nothing, and accepted, it showed the first part of a new lab. With two tabs open, an entry made in one was shown in the other, and an entry made in the second after that was added to the first's. Four stored records that do not fit (not JSON, another version, a look at a reading the logger had not taken, a status the runs do not give) were each set aside with the notice and kept under a backup key.
- **A logger run to its last reading.** After 120 presses of "Run on" with nothing put into the cup, the graph held 241 readings on one stop of the Tab key, End reached the last of them, the button under the graph stepped back from it, "Run on" said why it does no more, the page did not scroll sideways, and the part could be run again.
- **The calculator** opened from the notebook's card, worked a sum, and closed at Escape.
- **Sizes.** At 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768, 1280×800, 1366×768 and 1920×1080, in parts 1 and 3 with the cup worked and four entries made, the page did not scroll sideways, the cup's words were beside its drawing, and every button, field, choice and disclosure of the part was at least 44 pixels each way to a finger. The controls drawn smaller than that (the disclosures, the calculator's button, the warning's two buttons, the "Parts" button, Previous and Next) have the hit area of 44 pixels that the lab pages lay over a small control; the last four were measured at 768×1024. (The review measured the hit area of each control by asking the page what lies at each point about it, and found one short of that: the "Sources" disclosure at the foot of parts 2 and 3 was 40 pixels tall, the area of "Previous" in the row below lying over its foot. It has 4 pixels more room under it now, and measures 44.) The graph was 463 pixels wide at 768×1024 and at 1024×768 and 512 at the other sizes, which is as wide as it is drawn. With nine readings on it each reading's column was 33 or 36 pixels wide and as tall as the plot: narrower than a finger, which is what the two buttons under the graph are for. The list of parts was behind its button at the first two sizes and beside the part at the others.
- **The ways in.** Home, the list of labs and the search dialog each showed the lab and opened it, and opening it stored nothing. In a window 600 pixels wide the Lab tab showed the notice in place of the parts, with no Start.
- **Both themes** were loaded at 768×1024 and at 1280×800, and the browser's console was empty throughout. The page requested nothing but its own files.

**At the review of 2026-10-09,** on the build that holds its changes:

- **A whole Guided lab by the keyboard alone,** at 768×1024 in the light theme, from a browser with nothing stored: Start; the change before the lid and the stirrer in part 1, with the warning answered "Go back"; "Run on" until the notebook opened; every reading looked at with the arrow keys, Home and End; each entry typed or chosen, with ten entries first made wrongly (not written as asked, a temperature the logger does not show, a change that is not the difference, the salt left out, a signed heat, the water's change used for the metal's, a metal that is not the nearest, nothing chosen, a result without its sign, joules for kilojoules); the notes of each part opened; a reload of the complete lab; and the Record tab. Where focus was after each of these was read from the page: on the Lab tab's panel after Start, on the warning, on the lid after "Go back", on each button of the cup after its press, on the first reading as the graph's one stop, on "Change" after an entry is taken, in the field or the choice box after a refusal, on the sentence that says how the run came out once the worksheet is checked, and on the heading of the part after Next. For each part the script worked the entries out again from the readings and the bench's figures as the page showed them, and the page's own "how far off" agreed with them.
- **A whole Challenge lab by tap,** at 768×1024 in the dark theme, in a browser context that emulates a touch screen: Challenge chosen before Start; each part reached through the "Parts" list; and each part run twice, first carelessly (part 2 with no lid, part 3 with no stirrer, part 1 with four wrong entries) and then carefully. In the first run of each part every reading was looked at by a tap on its own column of the graph, 12 to 49 pixels wide by then, and each tap looked at the reading under it; in the second, with the "Later reading" button. At each stage of each run short of the check (untouched, the cup worked, every entry made) and after a check that did not count, the part shown was searched, in its markup, its attributes and what its fields held, for the stored figures, the metal, the relations, the words that say whether an entry follows, how far off a result was, and "given out" and "taken in": none was there, but for the worksheet's own marks once it had been checked. The search left out the Primer tab and a part that is done and not shown, whose notes are in the page as they should be.
- **By mouse,** at 1280×800: a whole Guided lab in the dark theme and a whole Challenge lab in the light. A pointer over each reading's column looked at it; moved down out of the plot through the same column, it left the callout on that reading; the callout was inside the picture at every reading tried.
- **The changes the review made,** each tried in the browser on that build: what a part finds under its title in both modes, with no word of direction; the bench's lines at the head of the notebook, and with the entry that waits in one window at 768×1024 at the first, fourth, sixth and seventh entries of part 2; the ring about the outcome, 8 pixels off the words at the sides and 4 over and under; a choice entered with Enter from its box, which leaves focus on "Change"; a change of a choice that Guided refuses, which stays open; a choice emptied by a change to the entry above it, which shows nothing chosen; the logger run to its last reading with no curve, where "Run on" and the notebook both say to run the part again; and a run of part 3 with the highest temperature typed 0.1 °C short of the logger's, which was taken at every entry, did not count, and said where to look. (Such a temperature is refused since 2026-10-10, and that sentence is gone: see "After the review" below.)
- **Contrast, measured.** Of the graph, from screenshots at 768×1024, as a ratio of luminances against the card: a filled point 19.8 to 1 in the light theme and 17.2 in the dark; the edge of an open point 14.9 and 13.6; the line through the points 3.7 and 5.1; the dashed line of the change 17.4 and 17.2; the ring about the reading looked at 19.4 and 16.6. The faint level lines behind the plot are 1.4 and 1.5 to 1, by the colours they are drawn in: a reading's value is said in its callout and is not read from them. Of the text, from the colours the page is drawn in, light and then dark: the callout and the axes' numbers and names 19.8 and 17.2; a figure read off the notebook 5.7 and 10.7; the label of the field that waits 5.0 and 13.0; a tick 5.6 and 10.3 on the card, 5.2 and 8.7 on the button of a step taken; a cross 4.8 and 6.2; the sentence that says why something cannot be done 5.1 and 8.9; and the grey text (what a button does, how an entry is written, the entries still to come, the key under the graph, "From the bench") 4.7 and 6.9, some of it 12 pixels tall. The page was not measured against any standard's levels as a whole, and this is no claim about them.
- **Hit areas, measured** by asking the page what lies at each point about a control's middle, on part 3 in the middle of its worksheet at 768×1024 and at 1280×800, and on part 2 with its worksheet checked at 768×1024: every control of a part 44 pixels or more each way, but for the graph's columns from the ninth reading (33 pixels wide at the first size and 36 at the second, as said below). At the head of the page, which the labs share: the mode's line 40 pixels tall, "Restart lab" 32, and the two links of the path over the title 18.
- **Sizes.** At 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768, 1366×768 and 1920×1080, part 3 with four entries made did not scroll sideways and had the cup's words beside its drawing.
- **Reduced motion, reloads, records that do not fit, two tabs.** With the operating system set to reduce motion the stirrer carried no animation and its marks were drawn; with motion allowed it was still 3.6 seconds on. A reload with the warning showing, and one in the middle of a worksheet with something typed and not entered; Restart refused and accepted. Ten stored records that do not fit (not JSON, another version, a look at a reading not taken, a status the runs do not give, a run whose index is not its place, a checked worksheet with entries missing, a key too many, an entry before the change, more steps than the logger takes, a metal that is none of the four) were each set aside with the notice and kept under a backup key. A step taken in one of two tabs showed in the other, and one taken in the second was added to the first's.
- **The ways in and the narrow page:** Home, the list of labs, the search and the About page; a window 600 pixels wide.
- **The shared form of choices, in another lab.** The review changed where focus goes after Enter in a choice box, and what a refused change does, in the form that the aqueous reactions lab and the flame test lab also use. One observation of the aqueous lab was made by keyboard after the change: answers that were not right left the form open with focus in the box; right ones left focus on "Change"; Escape cancelled a change; and a change to an answer that was not right was recorded and left the form open, as before. The flame test lab's forms were not tried at the review; they were on 2026-10-10 (below).
- The browser's console was empty throughout, and the page asked for nothing but its own files.

**After the review, on 2026-10-10.** The project's owner decided that a typed temperature must be the reading the logger shows, to the tenth of a degree (see "How entries are checked" under Faculty and curriculum), and the same assistant made the change and checked it:

- **The five scripts of the review were run again** on the build that holds the change, with the run that typed a temperature a tenth short changed to what the page now does: 264 checks passed. In Guided the highest temperature typed 0.1 °C short of the logger's, and the starting temperature 0.1 °C over it, were each refused at once, with the sentence that says which reading is wanted and focus left in the field; in Challenge the same temperature was taken and, when the worksheet was checked, was the one entry marked.
- **The flame test lab's forms, by keyboard,** at 1280×800, 11 checks. At one station: answers that were not right, entered with Enter from a choice box, left the form open and focus in the box; right ones disabled the boxes and left focus on "Change"; Enter on "Change" put focus in a box, with "Cancel" beside it, and Escape cancelled the change with focus back on "Change"; a change to an answer that was not right was recorded, the station went back to "recorded, not correct yet", and the form was open on it with no "Cancel". At the closing question: Enter with nothing chosen said to choose one; a wrong answer left the form open and focus in its box; the right answer disabled the boxes and left focus on the form's button, which then reads "Check answer" and is marked unavailable, because that answer completes the lab and a complete lab takes no change. So Escape and a change to a wrong answer could not be tried there.

**What has not been checked:**

- **No screen reader was used.** What is announced when the cup's state changes, when a reading is looked at, when the warning appears, when an entry is refused or taken, when the run's outcome comes or when the part shown changes has not been heard by anyone. The roles, names and states were read by script.
- **Chromium only, and no real device.** No other browser, no phone and no tablet. The taps were made by script in a desktop Chromium that emulates a touch screen; no finger has touched the page. The worksheet's fields ask a tablet for a keypad of digits, and the two entries that may begin with a minus sign for a full keyboard: which keys any tablet then shows has not been seen.
- **Contrast was measured on the graph and on the coloured and grey text of a part,** as given above, and nowhere else: not on the drawn cup, the bottles, the hazard tiles, the primer, the Record tab or the shared head of the page.
- Browser zoom, enlarged text and forced-colour (high contrast) mode have not been tried. No person with a colour-vision deficiency has looked at the page.
- Nobody has run the lab by hand, with a keyboard or otherwise. The only checks are those of the two AI coding assistants, the one that built the screens and the one that reviewed them, each by script.
- The window sizes tried are the six above and one 600 pixels wide, and no other.
- The hazard notes under the tiles were not read again against the safety data sheets at the review: the supplier's page for one of them was asked for and came back without the sheet's text, and nothing was done to get round that.

**Known limits of the calorimetry lab's page:**

- **A part is a long page.** With the cup worked and four entries made, part 1 measured between 2,300 and 2,410 pixels from its head to its foot and part 3 between 2,500 and 2,650, at the six sizes: a little over two screens of a tablet held upright, and more than three of a window 768 pixels tall. The bench, the cup, the graph and the worksheet are each needed while the worksheet is filled in, and the page folds none of them away. Since the review the notebook says the bench's lines again at its head, with the four metals' figures in part 3, so that an entry can be worked without going back up to the bench: by the fifth entry the bench's own card is 1,840 to 2,220 pixels above the entry that waits, and these lines 610 to 680. In a window 768 pixels tall the last entries and those lines do not fit one window, and are a short scroll apart.
- In the worksheet's numbered list, the number of an entry that is chosen (which way the heat went, which metal) stands beside its choice boxes and not beside its label, which is the line above. It is how the browser places the number of a list item that begins with a group of choices.
- From the ninth reading, a reading's column on the graph is narrower than a finger. The two buttons under the graph, each a finger's size, step one reading at a time, and a long run takes many presses of them.
- A mouse pointer that crosses the graph looks at every reading it crosses, as it does on the other labs' pictures, and a pointer that rests where the page moves under it looks at whatever reading comes under it.
- The callout of the reading last looked at stays until another is looked at. Escape takes it away only while the keyboard is on a reading; it lies over nothing else on the page.
- The numbers and the names of the graph's axes are part of the picture and are drawn smaller than the page's text: about 11.6 pixels tall where the picture is 463 pixels wide and 12.8 where it is 512.
- Changing an entry empties every entry under it, also when the same value is entered again. A line under the worksheet says so once two entries are made.
- The "Change" buttons of the entries that are made have the same name, and are told apart by the label of the entry each belongs to, which is each button's description.
- The part that is shown is not stored: a reload with two parts begun opens the first of them. A window made narrower than 768 pixels and then wide again, and a save made in another tab, draw the parts afresh in the same way, and what was typed and not entered is lost.
- The calculator belongs to the part that is shown: going to another part closes it and loses its sum.
- The mode's choice and "Restart lab" are the labs' shared head of the page, with what is said of it under the aqueous reactions lab above.

### The Group I cations lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built and checked on 2026-10-10 by the AI coding assistant that wrote them. Scripts drove the production build in headless Chromium 153 on Linux: 189 checks in four scripts, all passing at the last run. One step and the flow chart were driven by the keyboard alone (Tab, Enter, Space, Escape), two steps and the chart by taps in a browser context that emulates a touch screen, and the rest with a mouse pointer. The lab was then reviewed, the same day, by an AI coding assistant that had built none of it, with scripts of its own in the same browser: 443 checks in six scripts, all passing at the last run, which was made on the build the review left. What the review ran and measured is the second list below; what it found and changed is the last section of `docs/research/cations/README.md`. Neither set of scripts is part of the repository. No person took part, and how the lab looks (colour, spacing, proportion, the drawn tubes and the chart) is for the project's author to judge by eye and is not reported here.

**What the builder's scripts checked:**

- **A step by keyboard.** At 768×1024 in the light theme, step 1 of part 2 in Guided, from its heading. The Tab key went, in the order the page is read, to the hint, the two parts of the tube, the reagent, the centrifuge, the disclosure of the tube in words, and the pouring off. Enter added the reagent and Space used the centrifuge, each leaving focus on its button. Focus on a part of the tube looked at it; Escape closed its callout. Space chose an answer in each question and Enter in a choice box recorded them; Enter on the pouring off ended the step; the next stop was the step's notes, which Enter opened. Nothing is dragged, and nothing depends on timing or on a held key.
- **The flow chart by keyboard,** at 1280×800 in Challenge: Enter or Space on a tile put it in the place that was chosen; Enter on a tile's own button took it out and left focus on its place; the check was refused while a place was empty, and said why.
- **By tap,** at 768×1024 in Guided, steps 1 and 2 of part 1. A tap on a part of the tube showed what is seen there beside it; a tap on the other part moved the callout there, and a tap anywhere else closed it. In the chart, a tile tapped and then its place, and a place tapped and then each of its tiles, both filled the place; with one place alone to fill, a tap on a tile filled it; a tile that does not belong was refused with the reason, and so was one already there; a tile's own button took it out.
- **By mouse.** A pointer over a part of the tube looked at it and the callout was inside the picture; off the part it went, and the look had been stored. At 1280×800 a whole lab was run in Guided, both parts, to the line that says the lab is complete, then part 2 again.
- **Looking opens the questions.** With the reagent in, both questions were disabled, with what opens them said once under the first, and a look at each of the two parts opened them; that one look alone does not is held by an automated test. The centrifuge emptied both looks, and the two parts were listed as not looked at again.
- **Guided's three warnings.** A solid still spread through the liquid at the pouring off; the water of step 2 poured off cold with its solid still spread, which gave both warnings in one group; and step 5 recorded before litmus paper had shown the liquid acid. Each time the warning took focus and the press that raised it had done nothing. "Go back" put focus on the centrifuge, and at step 5 on the litmus paper; the way on poured, or recorded, and the run then said, for each slip, that it did not count and why. In Challenge the same pouring off was made at once with no warning.
- **Focus.** Where the control that had focus goes away at a press, focus was found on what comes next and not on the page itself: on the pouring off after Guided's check of a step, after the last tile that belongs, and after Challenge's check; on the line that says the step is done after the pouring off and after either button of a warning that ends the step; and on the sentence that says how the run came out after the naming of the ions. Next, the choice of a part and "Run this part again" each put it on the heading of the entry shown.
- **Controls that cannot be used yet.** A button that cannot be used stays in the tab order and is marked unavailable; pressing it says why in a status line beside it. Tried: the centrifuge with nothing to pack, the pouring off before anything is recorded and before the check, the check with a place empty, and the naming with no box ticked, which is asked again and sends nothing.
- **What Challenge and an unknown are not shown.** Automated tests draw the Lab tab at eleven points of a careful run of each part in each mode and search its markup for the notes and the equations of every step that is not done; none is there. They draw it at sixteen points of each of the seven unknowns of one seed, and wherever two unknowns have shown the same things the two pages are the same to the byte, so nothing in the markup says which ions a sample holds before they are named. In the browser, Challenge had no hint, said nothing of an observation that was not what the tube showed until the step was checked, and gave no count of the tiles that belong in a place; changed to Guided in the middle of a step, that observation was held to the tube. What the browser stores for the lab names an ion only where the learner named it.
- **Colour is not the only signal.** A look at a part of the tube says its colours in words, and the same words go to a status line. In the picture a liquid and a solid are each under a bracket with the word for which it is; a solid has an edge and a grain, and one that has not settled is drawn as specks with an edge under a bracket of its own. A part not yet looked at has a dashed edge, is listed in words as not looked at, and says so in its button's name. A step's and a part's standing, whether what was recorded is what the tube showed, whether a place of the chart holds what belongs in it and how a run came out are words, each beside a mark. The place of the chart that is chosen has a whole edge and the others a dashed one, and both it and a tile that is held are marked as pressed for a screen reader.
- **Motion.** Nothing on the lab's page moves: its stylesheet has no animation, which an automated test holds. With motion allowed, no animation ran on a step that was being worked. With the system set to reduce motion, none ran either, and the one transition found after a press, a button's change of background under the pointer, lasted a hundredth of a millisecond, which is the app's rule for every page; a moment later nothing was running.
- **Reload, Restart, other tabs, and records that do not fit.** A reload in the middle of a step came back on the Lab tab, at that part, that run and that step, with the tube as it was left; a reload of a lab that is complete with part 2 being run again came back on that run. Restart asked first: refused, it changed nothing; accepted, it showed the first step of a new lab. With two tabs open, a reagent added in one showed in the other, and the centrifuge used in the second was added to the first's record. Five stored records that do not fit (not JSON, another version, a pouring off with nothing recorded, a status the runs do not give, a key that names the ions) were each set aside with the notice and kept under a backup key.
- **Sizes.** At 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768, 1280×800, 1366×768 and 1920×1080, on step 1 of part 1 with its chart open and one tile in its place: the page did not scroll sideways; the tube's buttons were beside its picture; each part of the tube was 44 pixels or more each way; and every button, choice and disclosure of the Lab tab, 47 of them at the first two sizes and 52 at the others, was 44 pixels tall or more by its own height or, for one drawn smaller (a disclosure, the "Steps" button, Previous and Next, a place of the chart, and the button that takes a tile out, which is drawn 32 pixels each way), by the height of the hit area that the lab pages lay over a small control. That was read from the page and not measured by asking it what lies at each point about a control. The review did ask, and found that it was not so of the disclosures under the ways on: see the review's list. Every tile was 44 pixels or more each way at 768×1024. The list of steps was behind its button at the first two sizes and beside the step at the others.
- **How long a step is.** Measured at 768×1024, where 959 pixels of the window are under the tab bar: from the line over a step's title to the foot of its questions, 876 to 916 pixels once the questions are open, over the five steps of both parts, and 922 to 962 while they wait. In part 1 the chart is under that: a step's page was 1,699 to 1,881 pixels while its chart was being filled.
- **The ways in.** Home, the list of labs and the search dialog each showed the lab; the list opened it on its primer, and opening it stored nothing. In a window 600 pixels wide the Lab tab showed the notice in place of the steps, with no Start.
- **Both themes** were loaded at 768×1024 and at 1280×800 for a whole careful run of part 1 and of part 2, and the browser's console was empty throughout. The page asked for nothing but its own files.

**What the review of 2026-10-10 ran and measured,** at 768×1024 and at 1280×800:

- **Part 1 by the keyboard alone,** in Guided, from a browser with nothing stored. From a step's heading the Tab key went to the hint, the two parts of the tube, the reagent, the centrifuge, the tube in words, the pouring off, Next, the sources and the bench. Enter and Space worked every control; the chart was filled with the place chosen first and then each tile, and with the tile first. After the last tile that belongs focus was on the pouring off, or on the line that says the step is done; after Next it was on the next step's heading.
- **All seven unknowns, twice:** in Challenge by tap and in Guided. A careful run of each showed what its ions give and counted. At every point of the fourteen runs the Lab tab's markup, its accessibility tree and the Record tab's markup were kept, and wherever two unknowns had shown the same things so far the two were the same to the byte: 234 comparisons, none different. What the browser stored named an ion only where the learner had named it.
- **Each slip of technique alone,** in Guided and in Challenge. The warning took focus, and again when the same press raised it again; it is a group named by its title and described by its body; "Go back" put focus on the centrifuge, the bath or the litmus paper; the way on ended the step, and the run then said why it did not count.
- **Wrong entries.** Guided refused an observation that was not what the tube showed and said which part to look at again; it refused a tile that does not belong and one already there. Challenge took both and said nothing until the check. A naming of none was refused with its reason, and a wrong naming did not count and said what the sample held. An observation recorded in Challenge was held to the tube once the mode was changed to Guided, with a way on.
- **Reload, Restart and records that do not fit.** A reload in the middle of a step, and of a lab that is complete with part 2 being run again, came back where it was. Restart asked first. Eight stored records that do not fit were each set aside with the notice and kept under a backup key.
- **Hit areas, point by point.** For every control of the Lab tab, the page was asked what lies at each point through three columns and three rows of it. As first built, the disclosures under the ways on of every entry ("Sources", "The bench" and, in part 2, the map) stood 8 pixels apart, and the hit area of one lay over the one above: "Sources" measured 28 pixels tall, and with the bench open under it a click on the word "Sources" closed the bench and opened nothing; Previous measured 40, and a step's notes 40. They now stand 36 pixels apart and 28 under the ways on. On the build the review left, every control measured 44 pixels or more each way in twelve states of the page at each size: a step before its reagent, with it, with its chart open and a tile in, done, and done with its notes open; the whole chart; a warning; a step of part 2 with each of the three disclosures open; and the naming. The bottom of the tube, which is rounded as the glass is, measured 64 by 52 clear of its corners.
- **The picture's contrasts, on its pixels.** Drawn at four device pixels to a unit, and the same in both themes since the card is white in both. The brackets and their words are 10.4 to 1. The edge of a solid is 10.4 to 1 against a white solid and 8.9 against the colourless liquid over it, 8.2 against the yellow solid and 5.2 against the yellow to orange liquid over it, and 1.7 against the grey to black solid, which is itself 5.4 to 1 against its liquid. The edge of a speck is 5.2 to 1 or more against the liquid it is in. The grain is drawn at half strength and is under 3 to 1: 2.6 on a white solid, 2.5 on the yellow, and 2.9, drawn light, on the grey to black. The line of a liquid's surface is 2.5 to 1 against a colourless liquid and 2.1 against the yellow to orange. By colour alone the yellow solid and the yellow to orange liquid are 1.6 to 1, and a white solid and a colourless liquid 1.2.
- **The text's contrast,** from the colours the browser worked out for every piece of text on the Lab tab and the Record tab, light and then dark: the least among text that is not faint was 4.74 to 1, the grey words, some of them 12 pixels tall, and 6.21, a cross; a tick 5.2 and 8.7 or more; the sentence that says why something cannot be done 5.1 and 8.9. As first built, the words under the name of the part shown, and the number and the note of the entry shown in the list of steps, were grey on a grey ground, 4.35 and 5.86 to 1: they are now the text's own colour there, 18.2 and 14.5. The faint words of a button that cannot be used now are 3.71 and 4.86 to 1.
- **Motion.** With motion allowed, the transitions found just after a press were of a button's colours and lasted 150 milliseconds. With the system set to reduce motion none was caught on the last run, and on an earlier run of the same script three were, each a hundredth of a millisecond long. Four tenths of a second after the press nothing was running in either.
- **The other labs.** The shared list of entries was changed for the grey words above, and the shared tube and form of choices are this lab's too. One form of the aqueous reactions lab, of the flame test lab and of the calorimetry lab was worked by keyboard, with answers that are not right, right ones, Change and Escape. And the 36 lists that the shared list draws in the five labs were drawn on a build of the commit before the review and on the build after: they differ in the colour of the number and the note of the entry shown, or under the pointer, and in nothing else.

**What has not been checked:**

- **No screen reader was used.** What is announced when a part of the tube is looked at, when the tube changes, when a warning appears, when an observation is refused or taken, when a tile goes into a place or comes out, when a step is done or when the entry shown changes has not been heard by anyone. The roles, names and states were read by script.
- **Chromium only, and no real device.** No other browser, no phone and no tablet. The taps were made by script in a desktop Chromium that emulates a touch screen; no finger has touched the page.
- **Contrast was measured on the picture and on the text of the Lab tab and the Record tab,** as given above, by a script and from a rendering in one browser, and nowhere else: not on the primer, the bench's tiles or the shared head of the page. The dashed edge of a part not yet looked at was worked out from its colours and not measured: 3.4 to 1 on the card and 2.6 on the yellow to orange liquid. Over the grey to black solid of step 4 it could not be seen, which the review of the Le Chatelier's principle lab found on 2026-10-11 in a picture of the screen: since then an edge has a light line about it, in the shared tube of every lab, and the picture taken after shows the edge about the dark solid. A white solid and the white card are one colour and are parted by the edge alone. The page was not measured against any standard's levels as a whole, and this is no claim about them.
- Browser zoom, enlarged text and forced-colour (high contrast) mode have not been tried. No person with a colour-vision deficiency has looked at the page.
- Nobody has run the lab by hand, with a keyboard or otherwise. The only checks are those of the AI coding assistant that built the screens and of the one that reviewed them, by script.
- The window sizes tried are the builder's six and one 600 pixels wide, and no other; the review used two of the six and the narrow one. How long a step is was measured at two of them.

**Known limits of the Group I cations lab's page:**

- **A step of part 1 is two screens once its chart is open.** The tube, its buttons and its questions are about one window of an upright tablet, and the chart, with the tiles it is filled from, is a second under them. The pouring off, which ends a step, is under the chart and not beside the tube, so that it is where the learner is when it can be done. The centrifuge is beside the tube: once the chart is filled it is 925 to 1,037 pixels over the pouring off at 768×1024. A step's task has the centrifuge before the record, so a learner who follows it goes down the page once (as first built the task had the record first, and such a learner went up the page and came down again; changed at the review). A learner who records first, and one whom Guided sends back to the centrifuge, still makes that trip.
- **The grain that marks a solid is faint:** 2.5 to 2.9 to 1 on its solid, as measured above. A solid is told from a liquid by its edge and by the bracket with its word as well, which are 5.2 to 1 or more, and by what a look says in words. The review left the drawing as it is, for the project's author to judge.
- **A button that cannot be used now is drawn faint,** 3.7 to 1 in the light theme and 4.9 in the dark, as in the other labs. It stays in the tab order and says why when it is pressed.
- With the two parts' buttons and the "Steps" button over a step, and the tab bar at the window's top, the button that records what is seen is, once the questions are open, 14 to 54 pixels under the foot of a window 768 by 1024, until the page is scrolled by that much.
- **The flow chart is a tree of nested lists, not a drawing.** Each place is under the place it is made from, indented, with a line down its side. How a screen reader says the nesting has not been heard.
- The focus order and the reading order of a step are the tube, its buttons, the words under the picture, the questions, the chart, then what ends the step. Where the tube's card is narrow, the disclosure of the tube in words is drawn beside the picture's foot and comes after the words under the picture in that order.
- A mouse pointer that crosses the tube looks at each part it crosses, as on the other labs' pictures.
- The callout of a look lies over the brackets beside the tube while it is open, and its longest sentence stands just under the names under the rack.
- The small tubes of the rack are about 18 pixels wide and are not explored: what each holds is said in words in the disclosure beside the picture, and nowhere else.
- The names under the small tubes are part of the picture and are drawn in type about 10 pixels high.
- The picture of a step that is done is of its tube as it was when the step was checked, and the page says so; what that tube holds now is on the step that is being worked.
- The part and the entry that are shown are not stored: a reload opens on the part whose run is under way, at the step it has reached, whichever entry was being read. What was chosen and not yet recorded is lost by a reload, by a save made in another tab, and by a window made narrower than 768 pixels and wide again.
- The mode's choice and "Restart lab" are the labs' shared head of the page, with what is said of it under the aqueous reactions lab above.

### The Le Chatelier's principle lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-10 and checked on that day and the next by the AI coding assistant that wrote them. Scripts drove the production build in headless Chromium 153 on Linux: 186 checks in three scripts, all passing at the last run, and two more scripts that measured how long a disturbance's page is. One disturbance was driven by the keyboard alone (Tab, Enter, Space, the arrow keys, Escape), one system by taps in a browser context that emulates a touch screen, and the rest with a mouse pointer. The scripts are not part of the repository. The one review of the lab was then held, on 2026-10-11, by another AI coding assistant, one that had built none of it, working by script in the same browser and by reading: it ran the builder's three scripts again, ran its own, measured what is given as measured below, changed what the points below say was changed, and wrote what it found into the last section of the lab's research record (`docs/research/lechatelier/README.md`). So what follows was checked by the assistant that built the screens and again by one that did not, and by no person. No person took part, and how the lab looks (colour, spacing, proportion, the drawn tubes and baths) is for the project's author to judge by eye and is not reported here.

**What was checked that way:**

- **A disturbance by keyboard.** At 768×1024 in the light theme, the first disturbance of system 1 in Guided. From its heading the Tab key went, in the order the disturbance is worked, to the three choices of the prediction (one stop, the arrow keys moving between them), the button that records it, the hint, the two parts of the tube, the button that makes the change, the disclosure of the tube in words, Next, the sources and the bench; at 1280×800, where the tube is drawn on the left and the other cards on the right, the order was the same. Enter on the button that makes the change, pressed before a prediction, did nothing and said why. Space and an arrow key chose a prediction and Enter in the choice recorded it, leaving focus on "Change"; Enter opened it again with focus in the choices, Escape cancelled the change, and a second change was recorded. Enter added each of three portions, each leaving focus on its button, and a fourth press was refused with the reason. Focus on a part of the tube looked at it; Escape closed its callout. Nothing is dragged, and nothing depends on timing or on a held key.
- **The whole lab by the keyboard alone, at the review.** All seventeen disturbances in Guided, at 1280×800 in the light theme and at 768×1024 in the dark, with no pointer used once the lab was started: the Tab key to the choices of the prediction, Space or an arrow key and Enter; Tab to the button that makes the change and Enter for each portion; Shift+Tab back to the bottom of the tube and to the liquid, each looked at by the focus on it; Tab to the two questions of what is seen and to the question of the disturbance, the arrow keys and Enter; Tab to Next; and Shift+Tab to the next system's button. At the first disturbance of each system a wrong observation and a wrong answer were given first, and a wrong answer at each of the four tubes that ask what took the metal ion away: each was refused with focus left in the choices, and the right one then taken. After every action focus was where the point on focus below says. Each system counted, and the lab was complete: 304 checks at each size.
- **The whole lab by tap, at the review,** at 768×1024 in Challenge in the dark theme, in a context that emulates a touch screen: the change pressed before a prediction; a prediction changed before the change and no way to change it after; a fourth portion refused; nothing recorded refused; a wrong observation taken, changed before the answer, and a wrong answer taken once; the check, which said where the run did not count and not what was right; the marks then on that disturbance; "Work this system again"; a reload in the middle of a disturbance of the second run, which kept the portion, the look and the prediction; the three other systems, with what each look said held to the record at every portion; the lab complete; a system of it worked again and reloaded; and Restart refused and accepted. 128 checks.
- **By tap,** at 768×1024 in Challenge, the three disturbances of system 3. A tap on a part of the tube showed what is seen there beside it, and a tap anywhere else closed it. An observation that was not what the tube showed was taken and said to be not checked yet, and was changed by "Change"; a wrong answer was taken, and the disturbance's notes opened with the answer said to be not checked yet; the check then said that the run did not count and at which disturbance, and not what was right; and "Work this system again" opened a second run on its first disturbance with nothing chosen.
- **By mouse.** A pointer over a part of the tube looked at it and the callout was inside the picture; off the part it went, and the look had been stored. Whole labs were run, all seventeen disturbances to the line that says the lab is complete, in Guided and in Challenge at 768×1024 with the menu open and with it closed and at 1280×800, and in Guided at 1024×768; then a system of a complete lab was worked again.
- **Looking opens the questions.** With the prediction made, the card of what is seen was one line that said the change comes first, all of it; with every portion in, that both parts of the tube are to be looked at; and a look at each then opened the two questions, with no choice made. A press of the change emptied the looks, and both parts were listed as not looked at again. That a look made before the last portion does not open the questions is held by an automated test.
- **The prediction is kept and never marked.** Recorded, it was a line beside "Change" with no mark and nothing said of it; once the first portion was in it was a line that said it is fixed, with no way to change it; and on a disturbance that was done it stood over the notes as it was made, whether or not it was what happened.
- **Guided's refusals.** An observation that was not what the tube showed was not taken, and the form said which part to look at again and not what it should be; an answer that was not right was not taken, with a sentence that says how to look for it and not which answer is right. The right ones were then taken. Changed to Guided in the middle of a disturbance, an observation recorded wrongly in Challenge was held to the tube: the form said which part to look at again, and, since the review, the question under it took no answer, right or wrong, until the observation had been put right; before the review the right answer ended the disturbance there, and Guided's own check then said, two disturbances later, that the run did not count. An answer that is not right at a tube that asks what took the metal ion away has, since the review, a sentence of its own, which says to look whether the solid is still there and to think what went into the tube in a large amount.
- **Focus.** Where the control that had focus goes away at a press, focus was found on what comes next and not on the page itself: on "Change" after a prediction and after an observation is recorded, on the card of notes after the question is answered, with the notes in view, and on the sentence that says how the run came out after the check. Next, Previous, an entry chosen from the list, the choice of a system and "Work this system again" each put it on the heading of the entry shown.
- **Controls that cannot be used yet.** A button that cannot be used stays in the tab order and is marked unavailable; pressing it says why in a status line beside it. Tried: the change before a prediction and after its last portion.
- **What Challenge is not shown.** Automated tests draw the Lab tab at ninety-three points of a careful run of the four systems in each mode and search its markup for the notes of every disturbance that is not done and for what a look at the tube in hand would say; none is there. They draw every entry of a system that holds a wrong observation and a wrong answer, not yet checked, and find no mark on it. In the browser, Challenge had no hint, and said nothing of a wrong observation or a wrong answer until the system was checked; after the check the wrong answer carried its mark. What the browser stores for the lab is what the learner did, and nothing of whether it was right.
- **Colour is not the only signal.** A look at the liquid says its colour in words, and the same words go to a status line: "clear and violet", "clear, and between blue and yellow in colour". In the picture a liquid and a solid are each under a bracket with the word for which it is, and a solid has an edge and a grain. A tube in a bath is drawn in a beaker, with bubbles and steam and the words "boiling water", or blocks of ice and the words "ice water", and the line beside the picture says where a tube stands that is still in the bath of the disturbance before. A part not yet looked at has a dashed edge, is listed in words as not looked at, and says so in its button's name. The tube the learner is on, of the four of system 4 that stand together, is marked as current for a screen reader and is in bold, under a whole edge, behind a pointer; the list is named for the metal ("The four tubes of zinc") and each tube in it by what it is for. A system's and a disturbance's standing, whether what was recorded is what the tube showed, whether an answer is right and how a run came out are words, each beside a mark.
- **The colours of the liquids, measured on the screen.** One pixel of each liquid was read from a picture of the screen, in the light and in the dark theme, and each was the value the drawing gives it: blue #1d3ea6, violet #8f58d1, the colour between blue and yellow #00ae96, pink #f8b4ce, yellow #f6d93f, the liquid with no colour #e8edf2, on a card that is white in both themes. Worked out from those, as ratios of contrast: the three colours of the cobalt system differ by 2.0 to 1 (blue and violet), 2.8 (violet and pink) and 5.4 (blue and pink); the three of the indicator by 1.7 (violet and the colour between), 2.0 (the colour between and yellow) and 3.3 (violet and yellow). Across the systems the nearest are yellow and pink, and yellow and the liquid with no colour, 1.2 each; pink and the liquid with no colour, 1.4; the colour between and pink, 1.7. Against the white card: the liquid with no colour 1.2, yellow 1.4, pink 1.7, the colour between 2.8, violet 4.7, blue 9.2. A white solid is the card's own white, and is parted from it and from the liquid over it by its edge and its grain. None of these is a claim that any pair meets a threshold: a learner is not asked to tell any two apart by colour, since the word is said at every look. The review read the six values again from the pixels of the picture, drawn at four device pixels to the unit, in both themes, and found each as given. It also measured, on those pixels: the line of a liquid's surface, drawn at half strength, 2.5 to 1 against the liquid with no colour, 2.4 against the yellow, 2.2 against the pink, 1.9 against the colour between, 1.5 against the violet and 1.1 against the blue; the glass, 8.9 to 1 against the liquid with no colour, 7.4 against the yellow, 6.2 against the pink, 3.7 against the colour between, 2.2 against the violet and 1.1 against the blue, which is itself 9.2 to 1 against the card; the edge of the white solid, 10.4 to 1 against the solid and 8.9 against the liquid over it; the grain, 2.6 to 1 on the white solid; and the word beside a bracket, 10.4 to 1. The reviewer's judgement of the three pale liquids, from pictures of the screen set side by side and not from a person's eye: pink, yellow and the liquid with no colour are plainly three colours on the white card, in both themes, since the card is white in both; in a simulation of the three kinds of colour-blindness (Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes, 2009, at full strength) the least difference between two of the three was yellow against pink for a viewer without the blue cones, about three times a just noticeable one, and those two are never in one system. The faintest thing in the picture is the liquid with no colour against the card, 1.2 to 1, which the glass and the line of its surface hold.
- **Motion.** The lab's stylesheet has no animation and no transition, which an automated test holds, and the baths are drawn at rest. With motion allowed, no animation ran on a disturbance that was being worked. With the system set to reduce motion, none ran either, and the one transition found after a press, a button's change of background, lasted a hundredth of a millisecond, which is the app's rule for every page; a moment later nothing was running.
- **Reload, Restart, other tabs, and records that do not fit.** A reload in the middle of a system came back on the Lab tab, at that system and the disturbance it had reached; a reload of a lab that is complete with a system being worked again came back on that run. Restart asked first: refused, it changed nothing; accepted, it showed the first disturbance of a new lab. With two tabs open, a portion added in one showed in the other, and a portion added in the second was added to the first's record. Five stored records that do not fit (not JSON, another version, an answer with nothing recorded, a status the runs do not give, a key the record does not have) were each set aside with the notice and kept under a backup key.
- **Sizes.** At 768×1024, 820×1180, 1024×768, 1280×800, 1366×768 and 1920×1080, on the third disturbance of system 4 with its question open: the page did not scroll sideways; and each part of the tube was 51 pixels tall and 67 wide at the first two sizes and 58 by 76 at the others. The builder read every control's size from the page as the control's own or as that of the hit area laid over a small one. The review measured it by asking the page what lies at each point about a control: from the middle of each button, choice, disclosure and tab that was shown, how far up, down, left and right a point still answered to that control. Before its changes "Sources", under the ways on, answered over 28 pixels of height, and with the bench open under it did not answer at its own middle: a click or a tap on the word closed the bench and opened nothing. The sources and the bench now stand 36 pixels apart, as the Group I cations lab's do, and the click opens the sources. Six entries of the primer's list, those of one line with no note under them, were 32 pixels tall, in the shared list of every lab's primer; an entry is now 44 or more. After the changes 310 controls were asked about in nineteen states of the page at 768×1024 and 383 in eighteen at 1280×800, on the Lab tab, the Record tab and the Primer tab: every one measured 44 pixels or more each way but links in a list of sources, which are words in a line of text. The disturbance was one column at the first two sizes and two at the others; with the menu closed it was two at 768×1024 as well.
- **How long a disturbance is.** Measured over the seventeen disturbances in both modes, from the line over a disturbance's title to the foot of its last card, by the builder and again at the review, whose figures after its changes these are. At 768×1024 with the menu open, where 959 pixels of the window are under the tab bar: for the first three systems, 805 to 895 pixels before the prediction, 689 to 737 with the change made, 919 to 967 with the two questions open, and 944 to 974 at the question of the disturbance; for system 4, 835 to 931, 719 to 775, 949 to 1,005, and 974 to 1,042. With the menu closed, in two columns: 663 to 737, 576 to 662, 747 to 793, and 892 to 946 for the first three systems and up to 978 for system 4. At 1280×800, with 735 pixels under the tab bar: 604 to 690, 576 to 662, 667 to 773, and 692 to 826. As first built a page of system 4 was up to 1,214 pixels at the question of what took the metal ion away; the review shortened it by naming the three choices by the thing alone, so that they stand in one row, by listing the metal's four tubes in one row, and by putting Challenge's sentence that an answer is given once into one line.
- **The ways in.** Home, the list of labs and the search dialog each showed the lab; the list opened it on its primer, and opening it stored nothing. In a window 700 pixels wide the Lab tab showed the notice in place of the systems.
- **Both themes** were loaded at 768×1024 and at 1280×800 for a whole careful run of the lab, and the browser's console was empty throughout. The page asked for nothing but its own files.

**What has not been checked:**

- **No screen reader was used,** by the builder or at the review. What is announced when a part of the tube is looked at, when a portion goes in, when an observation or an answer is refused or taken, when a disturbance is done and its notes open, when a system is checked or when the entry shown changes has not been heard by anyone. The roles, names, descriptions and states were read by script, at the review from the browser's accessibility tree.
- **Chromium only, and no real device.** No other browser, no phone and no tablet. The taps were made by script in a desktop Chromium that emulates a touch screen; no finger has touched the page.
- **No person with a colour-vision deficiency has looked at the page.** The review worked the six values through a published simulation and looked at no simulated picture; a simulation is not a person. The six colours were chosen to differ in how light they are as well as in hue, and the figures above are ratios of contrast, which say how far two colours are apart in lightness and nothing of how they look to any one eye.
- **The contrast of the page's text was measured at the review and not before,** on the Lab tab and the Record tab in both themes at 768×1024 and 1280×800, from the colours the browser worked out for each piece of text and the first ground behind it that is not clear, in fourteen states of the page; not on the primer or the shared head of the page. Two things were found and changed. The number and the progress on the button of the system shown, 12 pixels tall, were the grey of the page's lesser words on the grey ground of that button, 4.35 to 1 in the light theme; they are now the text's own colour there and under the pointer, as in the shared list. "Not checked yet", beside an answer over a disturbance's notes in Challenge, was that grey on the tinted ground of those two lines, 4.49 to 1 by its colours; it is now the text's own colour. After the changes the least among text that is not faint was 4.74 to 1 in the light theme, the grey words on the page's ground, and 6.21 in the dark; the faint words of a button that cannot be used now were 3.71 and 4.88, which is the shared control's look in every lab and was left. In the picture, the brackets, their words, the bath's word and the edge of a solid are the ink of the other labs' tubes, 10.4 to 1 on the white card as measured. The dashed edge of a part not yet looked at lies over the liquid, in the ink at six tenths of its strength, with a light line about it. The line was this lab's own rule and is, since the review, the shared tube's, for every lab. Measured on the picture: on the blue liquid the ink is 1.1 to 1 and the light line 7.1; on the violet 1.6 and 3.9; on the colour between blue and yellow 2.2 and 2.4, the least of the six; on the pink, the yellow and the liquid with no colour the ink is 2.7 to 3.1 and the light line is not seen. The list beside the picture and the button's name say in words that a part has not been looked at. None of this is a claim about any standard's levels.
- Browser zoom, enlarged text and forced-colour (high contrast) mode have not been tried.
- Nobody has run the lab by hand, with a keyboard or otherwise. The only checks are those of the AI coding assistant that built the screens and of the one that reviewed them, by script.
- The window sizes tried are the six above and one 700 pixels wide, and no other. How long a disturbance is was measured at three of them.

**Known limits of the Le Chatelier's principle lab's page:**

- **A disturbance of system 4 is longer than a window of an upright tablet.** With the menu open at 768×1024 its page is up to 1,005 pixels with the two questions open and up to 1,042 at the question of the two tubes of each metal that ask what took the ion away, where 959 are under the tab bar; as first built it was up to 1,214. A disturbance of the first three systems is within 15 pixels of that window at every point of working it.
- With the four systems' buttons and the "Disturbances" button over a disturbance, which are 162 pixels, a disturbance of the first three systems ends 122 to 177 pixels under the foot of a window 768 by 1024 once its questions are open, until the page is scrolled by that much.
- **The page changes its length as a disturbance is worked.** A form folds to a line when its entry is made, a card comes when the one before it is worked, and a disturbance that is done shows its notes first, with focus moved to them. What that is like with a screen reader, or for a learner who is thrown by a page that moves, has not been tried by anyone.
- **A choice of direction names the way alone.** What stands on the left and on the right is said once, in the card of the equilibrium over both forms. Since the review each of the two choices that names a side is described by the card's line for that side: in the browser's accessibility tree "Toward the left side" has the description "Left side: lead ions and chloride ions, dissolved." Whether a screen reader says a description, and when, is the screen reader's; none was used.
- **In two columns the tube is drawn on the left and is second in the reading order,** after the equilibrium and the prediction, which are drawn on the right. The focus order and the reading order are the order of working at every width.
- **The three choices of what took the metal ion away name the thing alone,** "Hydroxide ions", "Ammonia" and "Nothing did", where each first said how the ion is held or that the solid is still there. That is the notes' to say, once the question is answered, and the primer's; the question over the choices says that what was added should give more solid.
- The Tab key goes through the two parts of the tube, and focus on a part looks at it, so a learner who tabs past the tube has looked at both. A mouse pointer that crosses the tube looks at each part it crosses, as on the other labs' pictures.
- The callout of a look lies over the brackets beside the tube while it is open, and over the beaker's wall where the tube is in a bath.
- **A drop or two does not raise the liquid in the picture,** or raises it by a pixel or two: that a portion went in is said in the line beside the picture, and each press empties the list of what has been looked at.
- **The other tubes of system 4 are not drawn.** The four of the metal in hand are listed over the picture, each by what it is for, and the picture is of the learner's own.
- The words in the picture, "liquid", "solid" and the bath's, are drawn in type 13 pixels high at the picture's full size and about 11 where it is drawn 280 pixels wide.
- The picture of a disturbance that is done is of its tube as it was left; what that tube shows now is on the disturbance that is being worked.
- The system and the entry that are shown are not stored: a reload opens on the system whose run is under way, the earlier of two, at the disturbance it has reached, whichever entry was being read. What was chosen and not yet recorded is lost by a reload, by a save made in another tab, and by a window made narrower than 768 pixels and wide again.
- The three ways an equilibrium can go stand in one row at 768×1024 because a choice of this lab's forms has a little less room either side of its words than the other labs'; with larger text they would wrap to two rows, which has not been tried.
- The mode's choice and "Restart lab" are the labs' shared head of the page, with what is said of it under the aqueous reactions lab above.

### The clock reaction lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-11 and checked that day by the AI coding assistant that wrote them. Scripts drove the production build in headless Chromium on Linux: 43 checks in two scripts, all passing at the last run, and three more scripts that took pictures of the page and measured how long its entries are. A whole lab was worked once from Start to the line that says it is complete, with a mouse pointer and, for stopping the watch and for the graph, the keyboard; a second lab was begun by taps in a browser context that emulates a touch screen, in Challenge, with motion reduced. The scripts are not part of the repository.

**The review.** On 2026-10-11 an AI coding assistant that had built none of the lab reviewed it, in the same way: by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build, and by reading. Nobody sat at a keyboard. It made the changes the owner had decided on (one speed for the five mixtures of part 1, the choice to have the watch stopped offered before the first pour, no press taken for half a second after a pour, and no strength in the starch bottle's note), put right what it found, and ran the lab again on the build that came of that. Where a line below gives what the builder found, the review found the same unless the line says otherwise; what the review ran is marked as its own. Its record is the last section of `docs/research/clock/README.md`.

**Changed on 2026-10-11.** The iodination of acetone lab's page draws pieces this page shares with it, and that lab's review, by an AI reviewer working by script in headless Chromium, changed four things in them, which are this page's too: "Work this notebook again" gives focus to the notebook's heading, where it left focus on the page's body; once part 1 is done its notes have more room under them, clear of the button of the list of runs at 768×1024; the two small lines of the part that is shown are in the page's own ink, where grey on its grey ground measured 4.35 to 1 in the light theme; and a stopped watch cannot read more than its limit, whenever its last frame comes. On that build the reviewer worked one run of each part and each notebook of this lab at 768×1024 and found the one speed of part 1, each bath's own pace, the choice beside the first pour and the half second after a pour as the lines below have them, and measured that the relation of a chosen entry stands 28 px under Enter with neither lying over the other. Nothing else below was run again.

**What was checked that way:**

- **A whole lab by the keyboard alone (the review).** In Guided, at 768×1024 in the light theme and at 1280×800 in the dark, from Start to the line that says the lab is complete: every button reached with the Tab key and pressed with Enter or the space bar, every entry typed, every choice made with the arrow keys, the graph walked with the arrow keys, Home and End. 76 checks at each size, all passing at the last run. After the press that pours, focus was on "Stop the watch"; after the stop, on the sentence that says how the run came out; after "Make this run again" and after Next, on the run's heading; after an entry was made, on its "Change"; after a refusal, still in the field; after the last entry of a notebook, on the sentence that says how the notebook came out. The builder had pressed the pour with a pointer in every run.
