- **What the watch records (the review).** A watch stopped by Enter about 0.35 seconds after the change was stored as stopped by hand with a delay of 348 to 390 milliseconds in six runs, each within 20 milliseconds of the script's own measure, by the page's clock, from the moment the picture changed; the time stored was the model's time of the change with that delay added, and the stopped watch read that time. The stopped watch reads the time of the press, which is later than the last figure drawn by up to one frame. With a watch running, the page drew 60 frames a second at both window sizes in both themes, the longest gap between two 27 milliseconds; in two of the six runs the script's own measuring slowed the frames, and the stopped watch read 0.3 seconds more than the last figure the script had seen.
- **A second press just after the pour (the review).** As built, a second press of Enter after the pour stopped the watch, since focus is on Stop by then, and the run did not count. The bench now takes no press for the first half second after a pour. Two more presses of Enter, 0.12 and 0.37 seconds after the pour, left the watch running; so did a second tap where the pour had been, at 768×1024, where the watch's card brings Stop, "Stop the watch for me at the change" and a disclosure under that place; and so did a double click on the pour at four places along it at 1280×800. A press is judged by when it was made and not by when the page came to it: in one run the page stood still for 2.5 seconds just after a pour, and a press made 0.17 seconds after the pour was still not taken. No run changes within 1.9 seconds of its pour.
- **The change is said, and not by colour alone.** At the change a status line read "The mixture has turned dark blue, all at once." where it had been empty; the words under the drawn flask and the sentence beside it read "dark blue" where they had read "clear and colourless". The watch has the role of a timer and is not a live region.
- **What the engine was handed.** A watch stopped by the Enter key about 0.3 seconds after the change was stored as stopped by hand with a delay of 299 to 383 milliseconds over four runs, and the run counted. One stopped before the change did not count and said that it was stopped before the liquid changed; one stopped more than 2 seconds after did not count and said so. Each was made again from its own button, with focus on the run's heading.
- **The second way of stopping.** "Stop the watch for me at the change", pressed while the watch ran, stayed pressed, the watch went on, and it was stopped at the change; it was still pressed at every run after it, to the last run of part 3. Pressed 1.2 seconds after the liquid had changed, it stopped the watch at the change, and the record says "at the change". The review: it now stands beside the pour before a watch is started, where it was pressed by Enter and by a tap before the pour and stopped that run's watch at the change; and pressed after the change it now stays pressed for the runs that follow, which as built it did not.
- **A run that is left.** With a watch running, the Record tab was shown and the Lab tab again; the page was told by the script that its tab was hidden, and then that it was shown; the next run was shown and the first again; and the page was reloaded. Each time the run was back before its pour with its starch in, the first three times with the sentence that says it was left and that no time was recorded, and nothing of a pour was in the stored record.
- **A flask with no starch.** In Challenge the pour was taken with no warning. The watch ran at its own speed for 20 seconds, ran on to 30:00.0 in 4 more (24.2 and 24.3 seconds in all at two runs, by the script's clock) and stopped itself, and the run said that no starch was added. The review: the Record tab said of that watch that it was stopped by hand; it now says that the watch stopped itself.
- **Guided's warning** took focus when a pour with no starch was pressed, and "Go back" put focus on the starch.
- **Motion.** With motion allowed, the picture carried its one mark as the liquid changed. With the system set to reduce motion, the picture carried none and the figures of the watch still changed. Asked at the moment of the change what was animating, the page answered nothing in four runs of five; in the fifth it answered one thing, which the check as first written did not name. The check now names what is running and asks again a moment later, and found nothing in two runs. The review asked in every frame from the change on, and found the one thing: with motion reduced the app gives every transition a length of a hundredth of a millisecond, and that held the liquid's new colour back by one frame after the words had changed. The liquid has no transition now, and nothing was animating in any of forty frames from the change on.
- **By tap,** at 768×1024 in Challenge: the starch, the pour, Stop (0.7 seconds after the change, and the run counted), "Make this run again", Previous and "Stop the watch for me at the change".
- **A whole lab in Challenge by tap (the review).** At 768×1024 in the dark theme, in a browser context that emulates a touch screen, from Start to the line that says the lab is complete and through Restart: 50 checks, all passing at the last run. A pour with no starch and a pour before the flasks had stood were taken with no warning and did not count; wrong entries of each kind were taken with no word on whether they follow; the check before every entry was made said what was missing; a notebook that did not count marked which entries did not follow and was worked again; a place on the graph was looked at by a tap on the plot and by the two step buttons. At nineteen points of that lab, on the Lab tab or on the Record tab, the text of the page was searched for what Challenge is kept from before a part is done (the model's orders, its rate constant, the sentence that gives the activation energy, the notes, whether an entry follows, a relation): none was there before its time.
- **The mode changed in the middle (the review).** A notebook whose nine entries were made in Challenge and not checked, with the mode then changed to Guided, said of each entry whether it follows and had nothing to press: Guided has no check, and nothing waited to be entered. The check is now offered there.
- **The graph by keyboard.** From the first place, the right arrow key twice moved to the third place, whose callout gave its two figures, and End to the twelfth. The places chosen in the notebook were then the two looked at.
- **Both notebooks,** in Guided, to the sentence that says each counts and to the line that says the lab is complete. The review made wrong entries of each kind in Guided, each refused with its own sentence and focus left in the field (a concentration left as the bottle's, letters, a figure short of three, a negative number, all of the thiosulfate taken for the rate, a rate by the minute, a wrong order, a rate constant with one order wrong, a wrong unit, a slope without its sign, an energy in joules), and changed an earlier entry, which emptied the entries under it. That the rows the notebook fills are not on the page before the row they copy is worked is held by an automated test.
- **Sizes.** At 768×1024, on a run before its pour, on a run whose watch was running and on a run of part 3 ready to pour, the builder read every button, disclosure and field of the Lab tab as 44 pixels or more each way, from each control's own size or, for one drawn smaller, from the size of the hit area that the lab pages lay over a small control. The review asked the page what lies 21 pixels either side of the middle of each control, each way, and found that reading wrong in one place: the disclosures under a running watch and under the ways on of every entry stood 8 pixels apart under hit areas of 44, so that "The mixture" and "Sources" each answered over 28 pixels of height, and with the bench open a click on the middle of "Sources" closed the bench and opened nothing. It is the fault the reviews of the Group I cations lab and of the Le Chatelier's principle lab found in those pages. They now stand 36 pixels apart. After the change, in fourteen states of the page at 768×1024 in each theme and at 1280×800 in the light theme, and with each disclosure open, every control of the Lab, Record and Primer tabs answered at each of those points, but for the twelve columns of the graph, which are 31 to 38 pixels wide and the plot's whole height, and have the two step buttons under the graph for a finger. The page did not scroll sideways, and neither did a table of either notebook.
- **How long things are,** as measured again after the review's changes. At 768×1024 with the menu open, where the page is 497 pixels wide and 959 pixels of the window are under the tab bar: a run of part 1 before its pour is 744 pixels from the line over its title to the foot of its last card, where it was 694, since the choice to have the watch stopped is a third row of buttons; with 194 pixels over it, it ends at 938 pixels; on the first screen of a lab that is not begun, 270 pixels stand over it and it ends at 1,014, so that third row is partly under the window's foot. With the watch running a run is 517 pixels, and stopped 385. A run of part 3 ready to pour is 594 pixels. At 1280×800, where 735 pixels are under the bar, a run before its pour is 667 pixels with 126 over it, 58 more than the window. Part 2's notebook's page is 1,220 pixels when it opens and 2,021 once it is done, and part 3's is 1,725.
- **Where an entry's figures are.** At 768×1024: the first mixture's table begins 267 pixels over the first entry of part 2 and 591 over the fourth, with the time the fourth is worked from under that table; the table the notebook fills ends 21 pixels over the first order and begins 591 over the rate constant; the first entry of part 3 is 253 pixels under the foot of the graph, and the slope has the figures of its two places in the two lines over it.
- **Reload.** A reload came back on the Lab tab at the first run that did not count yet.
- **Contrast, measured on pictures of the page (the review),** at 768×1024: the figures of the watch and its frame are 19.8 to 1 against their ground in the light theme and 17.2 to 1 in the dark; the line of the graph, a run's point, a place and its edge, the ring about the place looked at and the figures of the axes, which are drawn 13 pixels tall, are the same two figures. The drawn flask stands on a white card in both themes: the dark blue liquid is 13.5 to 1 against the card and 11.4 to 1 against the liquid before the change, which is itself 1.2 to 1 against the card, as a liquid with no colour should be, inside a glass drawn at 14.8 to 1. The faint lines beside the watch and the key under the graph use the shell's faint role, read from the browser's worked-out style and not measured again here.
- **Names and roles (the review),** read from the browser's accessibility tree: the watch is a timer named "The watch is running:" with its figures; the drawn flask is hidden from the tree, and the sentence beside it says the look of the liquid; the line that says the change is a status; the graph is an image named for what it is, with twelve buttons each named "Look at the line at 1/T =" and its figure.
- **Records the page never writes (the review).** A stored record poured with no stop was drawn with its watch at nothing and not running, and stopping it ended the run, which was made again. Four records that do not fit (a status the parts do not give, a watch stopped before it was started, a run the lab does not have, and text that is not a record at all) were each set aside with the shell's notice, and the lab started fresh.
- **The calorimetry lab (the review),** which now draws the typed entry that was lifted into the shell for this lab and uses the lifted keys: at 768×1024 its graph was walked by the arrow keys, Home and End, and by a step button pressed with Enter, and the worksheet of its first part was worked whole by the keyboard, with a refusal, Change and Escape, to the sentence that says the run counts. 9 checks, all passing.
- **The builder's own scripts** were run again on the build the review left, and passed: 43 checks.
- **Both themes** were loaded at 768×1024 and at 1280×800, at a run before its pour, with its watch running, at the change and stopped, at a bath's run, at Guided's warning, at part 2's notebook part worked and whole, at the graph with a place looked at, and at the Record tab; the browser's console was empty throughout.

**What has not been checked:**

- **No screen reader was used.** What is announced at the change, when a step of the flasks is taken, when a run counts or does not, when a place on the line is looked at or when an entry is refused has not been heard by anyone. In particular nobody has heard how long after the change the status line is spoken, which is what a learner who times by ear would be stopping the watch on. The roles, names and states were read by script.
- **Chromium only, and no real device.** No other browser, no phone and no tablet. The taps were made by script in a desktop Chromium that emulates a touch screen; no finger has touched the page. How late a real tablet draws the change, and how late it hands over a tap, is in every time stopped by hand and was measured on no device.
- **The contrast of the page's ordinary text was not measured again.** The review measured the watch, the liquid and the graph on pictures of the page, as above; the page's text uses the roles measured for the calorimetry lab above.
- **No person with a colour-vision deficiency has looked at the page,** and no simulation of one was made.
- Browser zoom, enlarged text and forced-colour (high contrast) mode have not been tried.
- Nobody has run the lab by hand, with a keyboard or otherwise. The only checks are those of the AI coding assistant that built the screens and of the one that reviewed them, by script.
- The window sizes tried are the two above, and no other.
- Pictures of the page took the script's browser many seconds while a watch was running, on a machine that was busy with other work; the page itself drew 60 frames a second there. What the page does on a slow device has not been tried. In the review, a watch started just after the page had been reloaded stood still for between 1 and about 22 seconds in the script's browser, which draws without a graphics card, in most runs that poured within a second or two of a reload; a trace of one such stall, of 1.9 seconds, put it in the browser's own disposal of the page before the reload and not in the page's script, and a run that waited 2.5 seconds after the reload did not stall. Each time the watch then read the right time of the change. It was seen on no device, and what a tablet does there was not tried.

**Known limits of the clock reaction lab's page:**

- **Timing by eye is the point of the lab, and not everyone can do it.** A run stopped by hand counts only if the press comes within 2.0 seconds of the change. "Stop the watch for me at the change" is beside the pour and beside Stop at every run, counts the same, and is kept once it is pressed, but it has to be found and pressed once. Until the review it was not offered before the first pour.
- **A learner who uses a screen reader hears the change after it is drawn.** The delay that is added to the time then includes the time the status line takes to be spoken.
- **The watch changes speed at the change.** Until then it runs 14 times as fast as a clock in part 1, and 5, 30 or 55 times as fast in a bath of part 3; from the change it adds true time. The page says that the time is played faster, at one speed for the five mixtures and each bath at its own pace; it does not say how much or that it changes.
- **The fourth mixture changes two to three seconds after its pour,** which is the point of playing the five at one speed, and leaves little time to find Stop; focus is already on it.
- **The same attempt changes at the same time each time it is poured.** A run that is left is poured again with its time unchanged, so a learner who has watched it once knows when to press.
- **Focus is on Stop from the pour.** For the first half second after a pour the bench takes no press, so that a second press of the key that poured does not stop the watch; a key pressed after that and before the change still stops it, and the run then does not count and is made again. A press that is meant and comes within that half second is not taken either, and nothing says so. A held key is taken as one press.
- **A press in the frame in which the page finds the change** is recorded with no delay.
- **The page changes its length as a run is worked:** the two cards of a run give way to one at the pour, and the buttons give way to how the run came out at the stop, with focus moved there. What that is like with a screen reader has not been tried by anyone.
- **The first screen of a lab that is not begun is 55 pixels longer than a window 768 by 1024,** so the choice to have the watch stopped is partly under the window's foot until something is done, and a run with Guided's warning showing is 106 pixels longer. At 1280 by 800 a run before its pour is 58 pixels longer than the window, and that choice is under its foot.
- **The list named Runs ends, in part 3, in the notebook,** which is not a run.
- **The points of the graph that are runs cannot be looked at:** their figures are in the table over the graph and in the graph in words. A column over a place on the line is about 34 pixels wide at 768×1024, narrower than a finger; the two buttons under the graph step from place to place.
- The Tab key stops once on the graph, and focus on a place looks at it, which is stored; a mouse pointer that crosses the plot looks at each place it crosses, as on the calorimetry lab's graph.
- The part and the entry that are shown are not stored: a reload opens on the first part that is begun and not done, at its first run that does not count yet. What was typed and not yet entered is lost by a reload, by a save made in another tab, and by a window made narrower than 768 pixels and wide again.
- The words in the picture are drawn in type about 13 pixels high under the mixed flask and 11 to 12 about the two flasks.
- The mode's choice and "Restart lab" are the labs' shared head of the page, with what is said of it under the aqueous reactions lab above.

### The iodination of acetone lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-11 and checked that day by the AI coding assistant that wrote them, by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build. Nobody sat at a keyboard. Much of the page is the clock reaction lab's, which the two labs now draw as one set of pieces (design spec, section 20.6): the navigation of the parts, the watch's card, the notebook, the Record tab. What is said of those under the clock reaction lab above was checked on that lab's page, and is repeated here only where it was run again on this one. The scripts are not part of the repository.

**The review.** On 2026-10-11 an AI reviewer that had built none of the lab reviewed it, in the same way: by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build, and by reading. Nobody sat at a keyboard. It made the change the owner had decided on (the speed at which the page plays a run is 60, a minute on the watch to a second, and the page says so), put right what it found, and ran the lab again on the build that came of that. Where a line below gives what the builder found, it says so; what the review ran is marked as its own. What it found and changed is the last section of `docs/research/acetone/README.md`.

**What was checked that way:**

- **A whole lab, at four sizes and themes.** At 768×1024 and at 1280×800, in the light and the dark theme, a script worked the lab in Guided from Start to the line that says it is complete: one run stopped by a press 0.3 seconds after the end, three stopped by the page, and the nine entries of the notebook, typed and chosen. In these the script held the page's clock and stepped it, so that a fade could be stopped at a chosen moment; the console was empty in all four. This was the builder's, and the review ran it again on its own build.
- **The review's whole labs, at the fade's own pace.** In Guided by the keyboard alone, at 768×1024 in the light theme, from Start to the line that says the lab is complete, and part 1 again at 1280×800 in the dark: a run stopped by Enter 0.3 seconds after the end, one stopped before the end and one stopped 5.4 seconds after it, each said why it did not count and was made again; the watch stopped by the page, chosen before the pour, kept at the next run and taken back; a run left three ways; and in the notebook wrong entries of each kind, the order in iodine among them, each refused with a sentence that gives no answer, then Change, Escape, the notes, the Record tab, a reload and Restart. In Challenge by emulated touch, at 768×1024 in the dark theme and at 1280×800 in the light, through Restart: two notebooks that did not count, each worked again, and one that counts. At 21 points of each Challenge lab the text of the Lab and Record tabs, with what is closed or hidden in them, was searched for the orders, the rate law, the rate constant and the notes of a part; none was there before its part was done. The console was empty throughout.
- **A run at the fade's own pace, by the keyboard.** With the browser's own clock, the pour was pressed with Enter and pressed again at once. The second press did not stop the watch, and focus was on "Stop the watch": so in ten of the builder's eleven runs, and in the nine of those that recorded when the browser had the two presses, they were 48 to 159 milliseconds apart. In one earlier run, which recorded no times, the second press stopped the watch. The review made that happen again and found it to be the script's: a script that waits for the page to take one press before it makes the next makes the second late when the page stands still, and with the page made to stand still for 0.8 seconds as it took the pour, that script's second press was made 0.85 seconds after the first by the browser's own times, outside the half second, and stopped the watch. With the same stall and the second press made 0.15 or 0.4 seconds after the first whatever the page was doing, the watch ran on, as it did for two Enters 45 to 49 milliseconds apart twelve times over, two presses of Space, a double click, a double tap, and Enter held down for up to 0.9 seconds. That the builder's one run was such a stall is inferred; it recorded no times. The mixture was drawn paler at each of the three words for its look. A watch stopped by Enter after the end was stored as stopped by hand with its delay, the run counted, focus went to the sentence that says so, and the stopped watch read the time stored.
- **How long a fade takes on the screen.** The review's, at the speed the page now has: by the page's own clock, from just before the press that poured to the frame whose status line first said the end, 5.72 seconds for the first mixture in a lab whose watch had the end at 341.3 s, which is 5.69 seconds at the one speed, and 11.40 seconds for the fourth, whose watch had it at 682.2 s, which is 11.37. The builder found the words under the flask and the colour to change in the same frame.
- **That Stop stays where it is.** The review's. At 768×1024 the watch and its lines stand under the picture and over the two buttons, and as first built Stop moved while the watch ran: 40 px down when the status line first said a step of the fade, and 8 px up at the end, so that a finger laid on the middle of Stop at the pour was over its upper edge by the end. The lines now keep their room from the pour, and Stop's upper edge was at one height at every frame of three runs at that size and of five at 1280×800, where it had not moved before.
- **A run that is left, one stopped early, and a reload.** A run left for another with its watch running had nothing stored and said so when shown again. A watch stopped while there was still colour did not count and said why, with the mixture drawn in the yellow it had at the stop; made again, the run was at its second attempt, with focus on its heading. A reload opened the Lab tab on the first run that did not count yet.
- **Reduced motion, touch, Challenge.** In a browser context that reports reduced motion and emulates a touch screen, in Challenge, by taps: nothing was animating while the watch ran, the colour and the watch still changed from one moment to the next, the watch that the page was asked to stop was stopped at the end, and the choice stayed pressed at the next run. The lab has no animation with motion reduced or without: the edge of the picture is not marked at the end, as it is in the clock reaction lab.
- **The colours of the fade, from pictures of the screen.** The mixture is drawn in a yellow that follows how much iodine is left: about #e7c755 for the first mixture 0.6 seconds after its pour, about #daa408 for the fourth, which has twice the iodine, and paler from there. Within 6 true milliseconds of the end it was drawn in #f2e596 beside water drawn in #e8edf2, and at the end in the water's own colour. Those two differ by 43.6 in CIELAB (the 1976 colour difference), and as a ratio of luminances they stand at 1.09 to 1; against the white card they stand on, at 1.28 to 1 and 1.18 to 1. The figures were the same in both themes, since the picture stands on its own white card in both. So the last of the colour is told from the water by its hue and not by how light it is, and a learner who cannot tell that hue has the words: the look of each flask is written under it in the picture, the look of the mixture is a sentence beside the watch, and a status line says the end, and before it a step down where there is room to say one (see the known limits). The review measured the same figures on its own build in both themes, and looked at the pictures: the end was plain in them. It also measured the two starts: the fourth mixture's against the first's is 1.36 to 1 and 19.7 apart in CIELAB, and the page has one word, yellow, for both.
- **Contrast of text.** The review's, from the colours as the browser composites them, in eight states of the Lab, Record and Primer tabs in each theme. The watch's figures: 19.8 to 1 in the light theme and 17.2 to 1 in the dark. One thing measured under 4.5 to 1: the two small grey lines of the part that is shown, on its grey ground, at 4.35 to 1 in the light theme. They are now in the page's own ink. After that every piece of text measured 4.5 to 1 or more in the light theme and 5.8 or more in the dark. A bottle's label is a drawing and was looked at, not measured.
- **Every control, point by point.** At both sizes, in the light theme, a script asked the page what answers at the middle of each control and 21 px from it each way: in a run before its pour, with the watch running and with it stopped, in the notebook with a typed entry waiting and with a choice waiting, and on the Record and Primer tabs, with every disclosure closed and with each open in turn. Every control answered for itself. One fault was found and put right first, in the notebook the two labs share: with the relation of a chosen entry open, its hit area lay over the lowest 6 px of the form's Enter. A choice was asked at its label. An open disclosure's summary was not asked under its middle, where its hit area does not reach, by design. The links in a list of sources are lines of text and were not asked. The hazards inside the bench were asked closed and not open. The review asked again on its own build, in those states and in 59 more at each size, the hazards and their sources open in turn inside the bench among them, a run that did not count, Challenge's notebook in four states, part 1 done, and each of the primer's cards. It found one more fault, at 768×1024: once part 1 is done its notes stand over the button of the list of runs, and a point 21 px under the middle of the notes' line was answered by that button. The notes now have more room under them, and every control answered for itself in every state at both sizes.
- **Focus after each action.** The review's, by the keyboard: on "Stop the watch" from the pour; on the sentence that says how a run came out when the watch stops, by a hand or by the page; on a run's heading when it is made again or come to; on the notebook's own outcome when it is checked. One fault was found and put right: "Work this notebook again" left focus on the page's body, and now gives it to the notebook's heading.
- **Names and roles.** The review's, by asking the page in seven states: no button, field, group of choices, table or region had no name; the picture is hidden from a screen reader beside its words; the watch is a timer and not a live region.
- **How long a run is.** At 768×1024 with the menu open, where 959 px of the window are under the tab bar: a run before its pour is 705 px from the line over its title to the foot of its last card, and ends at 899 px; on the first screen of a lab that is not begun it ends at 975, which puts the edge of its last card 16 px under the window's foot and no button. With the watch running it ends at 937 px from the pour to the stop, since the review, where it ended at 957 at the most, and stopped at 765. At 1280×800, where 735 px are under the bar: before the pour it ends at 724 px, and on the first screen at 800, where the pour and the choice beside it are under the window's foot; with the watch running, at 645.
- **By automated tests,** which run without a browser: the markup of the page, of a run before its pour, with its watch running and stopped, of the notebook and of the record; that the depth of the yellow follows the iodine left, is a yellow for any amount above nought and is the water's colour at nought; that the liquid has no transition and the picture no animation; that Challenge is shown nothing of what a result is held to before its part is done; and the playing of the time, without a clock.

**What was not checked:**

- No person has run the lab, by hand, by keyboard or by touch. No screen reader was used: that a step of the fade and the end are announced is known from the markup, a status line whose words change, and not from hearing them. The review cut what that line says so that the end is not held back behind a step (see the known limits), by reasoning and by the clock, and nobody has heard the result.
- One browser, headless Chromium on Linux. No real device, no real tablet, no real touch screen.
- How the colours look on a real screen, in daylight, or to a person with a colour vision deficiency. The figures above are of the colours as drawn.
- The page with the text enlarged, at 200% zoom, in a high-contrast mode, or with the window between the two sizes named.
- The Primer tab's cards one by one in a browser, beyond that they are drawn and that their controls answer. The review read their text.
- What a press's own time is in a browser other than Chromium, which the half second after a pour rests on.
- The review was by an AI reviewer, by script. No person has reviewed the lab.

**Known limits of the iodination of acetone lab's page:**

- **The end of the fade is drawn as a step.** A faint yellow is kept for any iodine at all and none is drawn at nought, so that the end can be seen on a screen. On a bench the last of the colour is hard to see, and the primer says so of the drawing.
- **The watch changes speed at the end.** Until then it runs 60 times as fast as a clock, for all four mixtures, and the page says so beside it: one second is one minute on the watch. From the end it adds true time, and that line is gone; the page does not say that the speed has changed.
- **The status line does not say every step of the fade.** It says the end always. It says the step to pale yellow in the first and the fourth mixture, where that comes 2.0 to 3.3 seconds before the end, and not in the second and the third, where it comes 1.0 to 1.6 seconds before; it never says the step to the faintest yellow, which comes 0.2 to 0.7 seconds before the end. The look is in words beside the watch at every moment. A learner who uses a screen reader still hears the end after it is drawn, and the delay that is added to the time includes the time the line takes to be spoken; the watch stopped by the page has no such delay.
- **The lines beside a running watch keep their room,** so there is a gap under them while they are short.
- **Timing by eye rests on a difference of hue.** A learner who cannot tell the faintest yellow from the water can have the watch stopped by the page, with one press that is offered before the first pour and is kept; a run timed so counts as any other, and the record says how each run was timed.
- **The second and third mixtures fade in 2.0 to 3.3 seconds.** A press that is meant and comes within half a second of the pour is not taken, as in the clock reaction lab.
- **The choice to have the watch stopped, pressed once the colour has gone, stops it at the end itself,** however late the press: a run that would not count for a late hand counts that way.
- **The fourth mixture starts a deeper yellow than the others, and the words under the flask do not say so.** That it holds twice the iodine is in its table.
- **A run left with its watch running is poured again,** and nothing of it is stored; the page says so when the run is shown again.
- Below 768 pixels of width the Lab tab shows a notice in place of the bench, as in every lab.

### The red cabbage pH lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-11 and checked that day by the AI coding assistant that wrote them, by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build. Nobody sat at a keyboard. The page is built on the pieces of the labs before it: the frame and its tabs, the test tube that is explored, the shared form of choices, the paged list, the tiles of the bench. What is said of those under the labs above was checked on those labs' pages. The lab's one deep review was held on 2026-10-12 by an AI reviewer that had built none of the lab, again by script in headless Chromium and by reading, and by no person: it made the change the project's owner had decided on (the meter's two buttons stay where they are under Guided's warning), put right what it found, and measured what the builder had not. What is the review's is marked so below; the last section of `docs/research/cabbage/README.md` has each finding and how it is known.

**What was checked that way:**

- **A whole lab, six times.** A script worked the lab from Start to the line that says it is complete as a careful learner would, from the page's own words: it read each tube of the rack by moving along it with the arrow keys, looked at each sample's liquid, chose on the small scale the tube with the same colour word, or that the indicator cannot say where no tube had it, rinsed the probe, read the meter, and answered the closing questions from the readings on the page. All fourteen things counted every time. In Guided and in Challenge, at 768×1024 with the menu open and with it closed and at 1280×800; the Challenge run at 768×1024 with the menu open was by emulated touch, with taps. The console was empty and nothing was asked of any other server. One run in six first failed: the lines that said what the part asks for went at the first press, the page moved up under the pointer, and the callout of the look closed. Those lines now stay until another part or thing is shown, and the six ran through after that.
- **The keyboard along the rack.** The rack is one stop of the Tab key. From the first tube the right arrow came to each of the thirteen in turn, and at each the line under the rack named that tube and its colour in words.
- **Every colour in words.** A look at a tube of the rack, in part 1 and beside a sample, and a look at a sample's liquid each say the colour as a word; the script worked the whole lab from those words alone. Before a look nothing on the page says what is seen in a sample. The review's: the callout of a sample's look goes when the pointer or the finger moves to the scale, and the words now stay in a line beside the tube once the liquid has been looked at.
- **The colours as numbers.** Each pair of neighbours on the scale that differ in word differs by 1.44 to 1 or more as a ratio of contrast and by 16.0 or more as a colour difference (CIEDE2000), and in lightness or strongly in hue; the two that share a word, pH 11 and pH 12, are drawn the same. These are worked out from the values the drawing uses (design spec, section 21.7, has the table). The review read the colours from the pixels of the page, in the rack and beside a sample, in both themes at both sizes: each tube is the value the drawing has, and the card under it is white in the dark theme too. The weakest pair of neighbours is the dark blue of pH 7 and the blue of pH 8, which differ in lightness and hardly in hue. The review also measured every two of the twelve colours, neighbours or not, with each of three colour vision deficiencies simulated (protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia, by a published model, at full severity). As built the purple of pH 5 came out as the blue of pH 8 with protanopia simulated, 1.6 apart, and the dark pink of pH 2 near the teal of pH 9 with deuteranopia, 6.8. Those two values were changed, and the least difference between any two of the twelve, as drawn or in any of the three simulations, is now 9.5; an automated test holds every pair apart in all four. A simulation is a model and nobody's eye. Against its white card the palest of the scale, the yellow of pH 13, is 1.41 to 1, and three looks of a thing before the indicator goes in are fainter still (1.18, 1.13 and none): a tube's glass, its surface line and its bracket are drawn in the ink, and the word says the colour.
- **Every disclosure's hit area, point by point.** At both sizes, in the light theme, on part 1 with a question open, on a sample with the estimate open, with Guided's warning up and once it counts, and on part 3, with every disclosure closed and with each open in turn (the hint, the rack in words, the sources, the bench, and inside the open bench its seven tiles' hazards): a script took the area a finger tall over each line of words, held it against the box of every other control, and asked the page what answers at each point of it, 3 px apart. No area lay over another control, and no point answered for another. Two faults were found and put right first: a hint and the rack in words stood 12 px under the button over them, and the area over an open one reaches 24 px up.
- **How large each thing to press is.** A tube of the rack is a button 65 by 113 px on an upright tablet with the menu open and never less than 47 px wide at the sizes tried; beside a sample, 47 to 65 px wide and 82 tall; "The indicator cannot say", 51 by 82 or 168 by 44; the liquid of a sample's tube, 57 by 83 or 76 by 110. Drawn smaller than 44 px, each with the 44 px area the frame lays over a small control: the lines that open a disclosure, the list's button, and Previous and Next.
- **How long a page is.** Design spec, section 21.7, has every figure. As the review left the page and measured it again: at 768×1024 with the menu open a sample is at most 927 px from the line over its name to the foot of its last card in Guided, where 976 px of the window are under the tab bar; the tube and the scale are in one window at every point of working it. In Challenge, with the meter read and the check to come, the longest sample is 990 px.
- **A reload.** Reopened the Lab tab where the stored record says the learner was; Guided's warning, which the record holds, was there again.
- **The review's whole labs.** One in Guided by the keyboard alone, at 768×1024 in the light theme and at 1280×800 in the dark, and one in Challenge by emulated touch at 768×1024 in each theme, each with the wrong answers a learner gives on the way: a wrong answer at the rack, a wrong tube, a wrong acid, neutral or basic, bleach with a tube named, the probe not rinsed, an estimate changed before the meter was read, all fourteen things, the closing questions wrong and then right, a reload and Restart. No press left focus on the page's body, once the one that did was put right: "Set it up again in a fresh tube" now moves focus to the thing's heading.
- **That the meter's two buttons stay where they are.** The review's, measured as the height of their upper edges down the page at each step, in each mode at both sizes. As built they moved 80 px down under Guided's warning at 768×1024 and 100 px at 1280×800, 20 px up when the meter was read, and stood 48 px lower after a reading taken anyway. Now the reading stands alone over them and everything that comes and goes is under them, and they were at one height at every step. A press within half a second of the one that raised the warning reads nothing: a double click, two taps of a finger and Enter twice each raised the warning and read nothing, and a press a second later read.
- **Every control's 44 px area, point by point.** The review's, wider than the check of the disclosures above: at both sizes, in seven states of the page, with every disclosure closed and each open in turn, the seven inside the open bench too, it asked the page what answers at each point of the 44 px area about every control, 4 px apart: 180 passes, 3.4 million points. No point answered for another control but in the open list of sources at 1280×800, under the known limits below.
- **The contrast of the text.** The review's, from the colours as the browser composites them, for every run of text in nine states of the Lab, Record and Primer tabs at 768×1024: 4.50 to 1 or more in the light theme and 6.21 or more in the dark. A control that cannot be used now is drawn at half strength and was not measured.
- **Motion.** The review's: with reduced motion asked for, no element of the page had a transition or an animation longer than a millisecond at five points of a lab.
- **Names and roles.** The review's, from the browser's own tree: the rack is a group of thirteen buttons named by their pH; beside a sample each tube's button is pressed or not, and "The indicator cannot say" is with them; acid, neutral or basic is a group of three radio buttons under its question.
- **Records that do not fit.** The review's: six were put in the lab's place in storage; each was set aside and kept, the page said so and offered Start.
- **By automated tests,** which run without a browser: the markup of the rack, of each of the fourteen things at each point of working it in each mode, of part 3 and of the record; that no thing's pH, note or mark is in the page before the meter is read, the sample counts or a check is made; that the colours of neighbours differ as above; that the stylesheet has no animation and no transition; where the page opens; and every sentence of the page.

**What was not checked:**

- No person has run the lab, by hand, by keyboard or by touch. No screen reader was used: that a look, a reading and a warning are announced is known from the markup, status lines whose words change, and not from hearing them.
- One browser, headless Chromium on Linux. No real device, no real tablet, no real touch screen.
- How the colours look on a real screen, in daylight, or to a person with a colour vision deficiency. The figures above are of the colours as drawn and as a model simulates three deficiencies; nobody with one has looked at the rack.
- The page with the text enlarged, at 200% zoom, in a high-contrast mode, or with the window between the sizes named.
- No chemistry instructor has read the record, the notes or the primer.
- The Primer tab's cards one by one in a browser, beyond that the tab is drawn.

**Known limits of the red cabbage pH lab's page:**

- **The lab is about telling colours apart.** A learner who cannot tell two neighbours of the scale apart by eye has the word for each at every look, and can work the whole lab from the words, as the script did; that is then matching words and not judging a colour, which is not what the lab sets out to teach.
- **On an upright tablet with the menu open the rack is two shelves,** pH 1 to 7 over pH 8 to 13, and not one row.
- **Dark blue and blue,** at pH 7 and pH 8, are the two nearest neighbours of the scale as drawn, and the two nearest of any two of the twelve. With deuteranopia simulated the purple of pH 5 and the blue-violet of pH 6 are the nearest, 9.6 apart, and with protanopia the purple and the blue of pH 8, 9.5.
- **A press on the meter's button within half a second of the one that raised Guided's warning** reads nothing and says nothing: it is taken as the first press come twice.
- **Guided takes a tube within a unit of the meter,** which may be the tube next to the one with the sample's colour, and then judges acid, neutral or basic against the meter: a learner who named pH 8 for a dark blue sample is asked for Neutral. The refusal sends the learner back to the colour.
- **In Challenge, with the meter read and the check to come,** the longest sample is 14 px longer than the window at 768×1024 with the menu open; the tube, the scale and the buttons are in view.
- **The list of sources, open, at 1280×800:** its links are lines 31 px tall one under another, and the 44 px area of one reaches 13 px into the next. The list is every lab's.
- **A tube chosen and not yet recorded is dropped** when another thing or part is shown.
- **A pointer that crosses a tube has looked at it,** as in the labs before it; the first tube of a rack with the indicator in that is crossed opens part 1's questions.
- Below 768 pixels of width the Lab tab shows a notice in place of the bench, as in every lab.

### The measurement and density lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-12 and checked that day by the AI coding assistant that wrote them, by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build. Nobody sat at a keyboard. The page is built on the pieces of the labs before it: the frame and its tabs, the paged list, the shared typed entry and form of choices, Guided's warning, the hazards' tiles and the calculator. What is new is the drawing of a scale and its closer look. The one deep review of the lab was held on 2026-10-12 by an AI reviewer that had built none of it, working by script in headless Chromium on the production build and by reading; what it ran and measured is under its own heading below, after what the builder checked.

**What was checked that way:**

- **How far apart two marks of a closer look are.** The lab asks a learner to see which two marks an edge stands between and to judge tenths of the gap, so the gap was measured on the page. At 768×1024 with the menu open: 76.8 px on the centimetre ruler; 76.8, 65.9 or 57.6 px on the millimetre ruler, whose closer look has five gaps, six or seven (seeds 1 to 40, both sides of the note); 69.5 px in the cylinder and on the thermometer. With the menu closed, and at 1280×800: 85.0 px on the rulers at five gaps, and the same 69.5 px upright. A reading counts within two tenths of a gap of where the edge stands, which is 11 px or more at every one of these.
- **Every instrument in words.** Each instrument's page has, behind a closed disclosure, where its edge, its level or its thread stands: the two marks it is between and whereabouts between them, in seven coarse steps, and where the eye is. The same words are the description of its pictures. They never give the reading; an automated test makes a reading from the words alone for every scale and every position of the eye at seeds 1 to 100 and holds that it counts. The balance's words give its display, which is read by copying it.
- **The closer look by the keyboard and by touch.** It is opened and closed by a button, which Enter and Space worked and which kept focus; an emulated tap opened it. Nothing on the page depends on a pointer resting on it.
- **That buttons stay where they are.** Measured as the place of each on the page: the button of the closer look before and after it opens; the three buttons that move the eye and the button that records a reading, through every move of the eye, with the closer look open; the button that records a reading, when a reading is refused and when Guided's warning comes up under it; the two buttons that put the pieces in, when the warning comes up and when the pieces are in. None moved. Everything that comes and goes is under the button that brings it.
- **The eye.** Three buttons, the one where the eye is pressed, which is said to assistive technology, and marked with a filled dot beside two open ones; a sentence says what is seen from there. The eye is drawn beside the cylinder and the level where it looks to be from there.
- **A reading refused, and Guided's warning.** A level typed with too few figures and with too many was refused under its field with the reason. A level typed with the eye above it raised the warning, which took focus; the way back put focus on "Bring your eye level", and the way on recorded the level and marked it as not as shown, with why. In part 3, pieces put in unweighed raised the warning, and dropped in unweighed raised one warning that named both; the way back put focus on the button that was pressed.
- **A part by typing.** A script worked part 1 in Guided from the page as a careful learner would, each reading typed at its instrument and each entry in the notebook, with an area rounded to a figure too many refused on the way with the rule it broke. At the last entry the notebook's page was shown and the line that says the run counts had focus.
- **A reload.** Reopened the part and the page of it the stored record says the learner was at.
- **Every disclosure's hit area, point by point.** At 768×1024 in the light theme, on the bench of each part, on the cylinder's page of parts 1 and 3, on the notebooks of parts 1 and 2 in Guided and in Challenge, and on a run that counts, with every disclosure closed and with each open in turn (the hazards of each tile and the sources inside them, the bench in words, an instrument's words, each relation, the notes, the sources): a script took the area a finger tall over each and asked the page what answers at points 3 px apart. As first built two areas reached a neighbour: an instrument's words, open, reached 12 px into the field and the button over them, and the bench in words reached the last tile's hazards. Both were given room, and no point then answered for another control.
- **How large each thing to press is.** On those pages no button, field or choice was under 44 px either way, apart from the controls that carry the frame's own 44 px area (a disclosure's line, the list's button, Previous and Next, the calculator's button, the two buttons of a warning).
- **How long a page is.** At 768×1024 with the menu open, 976 px of the window are under the tab bar. With its closer look open an instrument's page is 746 to 899 px in part 1 (the thermometer's and the millimetre ruler's); in part 3 the cylinder's is 964 px with the pieces in and the closer look open, inside the window. As first built it was 984 px, 8 px more than the window has: the review made the cylinder's closer look 400 px tall where it was 420, with less room beyond its first and last marks and its marks as far apart as before, and the cylinder's page of part 1 is 778 px where it was 798. Part 1's notebook is 880 px and part 2's 1,017 px in Guided, where the density's field is 364 px under the first reading and the last choice of a name 391 px under the top of its table. Design spec, section 22.7, has every figure.
- **Challenge.** With every reading taken, the notebook said nothing of whether a reading was as shown, of how many figures it had, or of what relation an entry uses; "Check the notebook" was offered and said what was missing. An automated test holds the same of every page of the part.
- **Motion.** With reduced motion asked for, no element of the page had a transition or an animation longer than a millisecond, at three points of a lab; with it not asked for, ten did, all of them the frame's buttons. The lab has no animation of its own.
- **Both themes, two sizes.** Pictures of twenty-three states of the page were taken at 768×1024 and at 1280×800, in the light and the dark theme, with no error reported by the browser; the page did not scroll sideways at either size. The assistant that built the page looked at fourteen pictures: twelve of the set as it was first taken, and two of the set taken again after the last change to the page, which took the number off the choice of a name. Among them were each kind of scale with its closer look, the cylinder with the eye off the level and Guided's warning, the pieces in the cylinder and on the balance, a notebook with an entry refused, the table of candidates, a run checked in Challenge, the bench's tiles and the record. The other pictures were taken and not looked at.
- **By automated tests,** which run without a browser: the marks of every scale; that every closer look holds a numbered mark, and that an edge is drawn where it stands between its two marks, at seeds 1 to 100; that a picture prints no number but those its scale prints; that no page prints a reading of a scale before it is typed; that the pieces of metal are drawn alike for all three metals; that every page of parts 2 and 3 names each thing an unknown may be as often whichever it is; that Challenge shows nothing of an entry before the check; where the page opens; and the markup of each page.

**What the review of 2026-10-12 ran and measured,** on the build as it left it:

- **Two whole labs, each scale read from its picture.** One in Guided by the keyboard alone, at 1280×800 in the dark theme: every page reached with Tab and Enter, every reading typed, each entry made. One in Challenge by emulated touch, at 768×1024 in the light theme. Each from a new lab to the line that says the lab is complete, in stages over the stored record. The reviewer took a picture of each closer look, read it before looking at what the engine held, and typed what it read: 14 readings of the centimetre ruler, 14 of the millimetre ruler, 15 of the cylinder with the eye level and 7 of the thermometer, at seven seeds; all 50 were within the allowance of two in the last figure, and each density came out at the stored figure. The reviewer is an AI and placed each edge by where it stood in the picture: this is not a person's eye at a screen.
- **What can go wrong, made to go wrong.** A reading with too few figures at each instrument, and with too many at each but the thermometer; a level typed from above in Guided, which raised the warning, and one from below in Challenge, which was taken and marked when the notebook was checked; entries with a figure too many, one too few and one cut off, a difference with a decimal place too many and too few and with the wrong sign; a density upside down, one with the cylinder's mass not taken off, one over the last level; the pieces dropped in and weighed wet, in Challenge with no warning and in Guided through the warning; the wrong liquid and the wrong metal named; a step taken before the reading it would lose; a part run again; a reload at each stage; Restart, which asks first. Each was refused, warned of or marked with its own sentence, and none left a part that could not be finished, but for the one below.
- **What it found and changed.** Part 1 could not be finished where the centimetre ruler's area came to a whole number of tens, about one seed in ten: "70" was refused as having one figure and "70.0" as having three. The notebook now takes it, and the primer says why. The metals' hazard notes gave how many grams of each metal the bench holds; they no longer do. The cylinder's page of part 3 was 8 px longer than a screen and is 12 px inside it. Under an empty cylinder the page spoke of the liquid's surface, and of a first level it said that the pieces "are in the water now" after they had been taken out; both sentences are put right.
- **Nothing names an unknown before it is named.** At eight seeds that have both liquids and all three metals, in both modes, on every page of parts 2 and 3 with every disclosure open and on the Record tab, the names and formulas of the things an unknown may be were counted in the page's markup; the counts were the same at every seed.
- **Focus,** after each thing pressed: a page's heading after Next and Previous; a part's heading after another part and after a new run; the button itself after the closer look and after a move of the eye; the warning when it comes; "Bring your eye level" after "Go back" from a level, and the button that was pressed after "Go back" from a step; an entry's Change button once it is made; how the run came out after the check.
- **Every disclosure's hit area and the size of everything to press,** again on the changed build, as the builder measured them: no point answered for another control, and nothing was under 44 px but what carries the frame's own area.
- **Contrast,** from the page's computed colours, in the light and the dark theme. The text is 4.7 to 1 or more in the light theme (the smallest, the grey 12 px numbers of the three parts) and 6.2 to 1 or more in the dark, apart from the text of a control that cannot be used, which the frame draws at half strength: 3.7 to 1 and 4.9 to 1. The marks and the numbers of every scale are 19.8 to 1 and 19.0 to 1; the unit beside a scale 9.3 and 10.7 to 1; the curve of a liquid 4.4 and 6.0 to 1 against the picture's ground and 3.5 and 4.9 to 1 against the liquid under it; the thermometer's thread 9.3 and 10.7 to 1. No conformance to any standard is claimed.
- **Motion,** again: none with reduced motion asked for.
- **The three labs that share the rounding code.** The lab added rules to the file the calorimetry, clock reaction and iodination of acetone labs take their three-figure rule from, and removed none of its lines. In each of those labs one worked-out entry was made in the browser: it was refused at the wrong count of figures and taken at three, as before.

**What was not checked:**

- No person has run the lab, by hand, by keyboard or by touch. No screen reader was used: that a refusal, a warning and a run's outcome are announced is known from the markup and from where focus went, and not from hearing them.
- One browser, headless Chromium on Linux. No real device, no real tablet, no real touch screen.
- Whether a person can judge tenths of the gaps measured above, on a real screen, at arm's length. The figures are of the page; no person has read a scale from it. The review's fifty readings were an AI's, from pictures.
- The page with the text enlarged, at 200% zoom, in a high-contrast mode, or with the window between the sizes named.
- A whole lab in one sitting. The builder drew parts 2 and 3 from stored records made by the engine's own test script; the review worked each whole lab by pressing and typing, in stages, each stage a script that reopened the stored record and went on.
- The hazard notes against the documents they rest on, by anyone but the assistant that wrote them.
- No chemistry instructor has read the record, the notes or the primer.

**Known limits of the measurement and density lab's page:**

- **Reading a scale is seeing.** A learner who cannot see the drawing has the two marks and the whereabouts between them in words, in seven coarse steps, and can make every reading from them; that is then choosing a figure for a phrase, and not judging a gap by eye.
- **The millimetre ruler's closer look is its smallest:** 57.6 px between marks on an upright tablet with the menu open, where it shows seven gaps to reach a numbered mark.
- **The picture of a ruler on the bench is small:** its millimetre marks stand 2 px apart there and cannot be counted. The closer look is the picture that is read.
- **At 1280×800 an instrument's page with its closer look open is longer than the window** (726 to 918 px, with 752 under the tab bar).
- **What is typed and not yet recorded is dropped,** and a closer look is closed, when another page or part is shown.
- **A level held back by Guided's warning empties its field:** from where the eye is brought to, the level is read and typed afresh.
- **Previous and Next are at the foot of a page,** so they stand at another height from one page to the next, as in every lab that has the paged list.
- **The pieces of metal are drawn alike,** grey, whichever metal they are. Real copper would be known at a glance.
- Below 768 pixels of width the Lab tab shows a notice in place of the bench, as in every lab.

### The freezing point depression lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-12 and checked that day by the AI coding assistant that wrote them, by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build. Nobody sat at a keyboard, a mouse or a touch screen. The lab's one deep review was held on the same day, by an AI reviewer that had built none of the lab, working by script in the same headless Chromium and by reading. What it ran is given below beside what the builder ran, each point saying whose it is; what it found and changed is the last section of `docs/research/freezing/README.md`.

**What was checked that way:**

- **A whole lab by the keyboard alone, and one by touch (the review).** The review's script worked the lab from Start to the line that says it is complete four times: in Guided with the Tab key, Enter, the arrow keys and typing and no pointer, at 768×1024 in the light theme before its changes and after them and at 1280×800 in the dark theme; and in Challenge by emulated touch at 768×1024. Every control was reached by the Tab key; the graph's readings were walked with Home and the arrow keys, and the liquid was chosen with the arrow keys. Every entry was worked from what the page showed. Every run counted. Part 3 was carried to its end with each of the three salts: sodium chloride twice, potassium chloride twice, and sodium bromide once, as a third run of the part in a lab already complete. One lab's two temperatures and two weighings were worked again by hand to its three results, and agreed.
- **A whole lab by typing, in each mode (the builder).** A script worked the lab from Start to the line that says it is complete: in Guided at 768×1024 and in Challenge at 1280×800. It pressed the cup's steps, pressed Run on until the last three readings it looked at were the same, looked at the two readings the notebook asks for and typed what their callouts said, read the two masses from the head of the notebook, and worked every entry from those to three figures. Every run counted, in both modes; the unknown was named rightly; part 3 was run with urea and with sodium chloride. After the lab was complete a third run of part 3 was begun with potassium chloride, and a reload reopened the page on part 3 with that run.
- **What can go wrong, made to go wrong,** in Guided: the logger started with the lid off and the stirrer still; the reading of part 1 entered at once, before the cup had levelled off; the solute added with the lid off; Run on with a little ice left; the cup strained with no ice left; and the solution weighed without straining. Each raised its warning, which took focus; "Go back" put focus on the step that was skipped (the lid, the strainer) or on the field; "Go on anyway" did what was pressed; the reading of part 1 that was entered so gave a run that did not count, and said why. A press of a button that could not be used said why in its own card. A temperature that was not the logger's, and one that was no number, were each refused under the field with the reason. A change to Challenge dropped a warning that was showing, and the same press then went on with no question. The review ran the same script again on the page as it now is, and beside it: that Run on warns of little ice once in a run (one warning; "Go back", which put focus on Run on; the warning again at the next press; "Go on anyway"; then four presses with a little ice still left and no warning); the wrong liquid named, which Guided refused without naming the right one and Challenge took and marked at the check; a stored record that did not fit, three ways, each set aside and said to be; Restart, refused and accepted; and a notebook filled in Challenge with the mode then changed to Guided, which had no button to check it and now has.
- **The ice without colour.** With the lid off the cup went from plenty of ice to a little to none: the drawing showed six cubes, two, and none, and the status line beside it said each in words.
- **The graph by pointer, by emulated touch and by the keyboard.** A tap on a reading and a click on one each showed its callout, with the time and the temperature to a hundredth of a degree. The right arrow, Home and End moved from reading to reading and looked at each; Escape took the callout away; the two buttons under the graph stepped. The graph was one stop of the Tab key. With seventeen readings a column was 22 px wide at 768×1024, so the two buttons, a finger's size each, are how a reading is reached by touch there.
- **That Run on stays under the hand.** Its place on the page was measured before and after every press of each run of presses, in the three parts, in both modes: one place each time.
- **Every disclosure's hit area again, those inside others among them (the review).** The builder's pass opened each disclosure that was in view and so never the sources inside an open hazard tile. The review's opened every disclosure of the panel in turn with the ones it lies inside, on six states of the page (four tiles, three tiles, a run just weighed, a finished run of each part), at both sizes with emulated touch: up to twelve disclosures on a page, six of them inside another. No point of any control's area answered for another control, the least room about a laid area or a link was 4 px, and no button, field or summary was under 44 px with the frame's area counted. The links in a list of sources are the height of their text: 31 px where a citation is one line.
- **Every disclosure's hit area, point by point (the builder).** At 768×1024 and at 1280×800 with emulated touch, on five states of the page (a notebook that waits, with its relation; a finished run of each part), with every disclosure closed and then with each open in turn: every point of the 44 px area of each control was asked which control answers there. None answered for another, and the least room between a laid area and the next control was 4 px. No button, field or choice box was under 44 px either way, apart from the controls that carry the frame's own 44 px area and the links in a list of sources.
- **How long a run's page is, before the review's changes and after.** At 768×1024 with the menu open, 959 px of the window are under the tab bar. As built, the cup's card and the graph's were together 924 px in part 1 and in part 3 with urea and 974 px in part 2, where the step that adds the solute took a row of its own; the whole page of a finished run was 3,003 to 3,198 px as the review measured it. Now the cup's five steps are two rows in every part and the two cards are 924 px, and 945 px once the cup is strained and the graph's key has a line more. Once the solution is weighed the bench, the cup and the straining are each folded under one line, 90 px each where they were 601 to 664, 328 to 378 and 120: the page is 2,268 to 2,288 px as the notebook opens, 2,672 to 2,714 px with its eighth entry waiting (it was 3,494 to 3,584), and 2,246 to 2,326 px when the run is finished. The card in which the solution is weighed stood at one place in the window through the fold in every run the review worked, to the pixel at 768×1024 and within one at 1280×800, and focus went to that card's line. The bench's figures at the head of the notebook and the entry that waits are in one screen at every entry, 913 px at the most as the builder measured, which the fold does not change.
- **Motion.** One thing moves: the drawn stirrer bobs four times, for under four seconds, as the drawing first shows it going. With reduced motion asked for, no element of the page had an animation. The review pressed Run on, looked at a reading, added the solute, opened a disclosure and moved focus with reduced motion asked for and listed what the browser was animating: the stirrer was not, and every transition had a length of a hundredth of a millisecond. Without it, the stirrer's bob and the fade of a pressed button's colours over 150 ms were all there was.
- **Both themes, two sizes.** Pictures of twenty states of the page were taken at 768×1024 and at 1280×800, in the light and the dark theme, eighty in all, with no error reported by the browser; the page did not scroll sideways at either size. The builder looked at twelve of the eighty: the cup with plenty of ice, with a little, with none, and strained in each theme; the graph falling and with a point looked at; a warning; the notebook part worked; the choice of salt; the Record tab; and the whole page of a finished run of part 3. Six of the twelve were of the build before the last change to the page, which put the lid of a poured-out cup on the bench. The other sixty-eight were taken and not looked at. The review retook the forty that its changes altered (ten states at each size in each theme) and took four of a state the fold made, a run just weighed with the cup's card opened again. It looked at seven pictures: two of the builder's, the level graph in the light theme and a reading looked at in the dark; and five of its own, the whole page of a finished run at 768×1024 in the light theme and at 1280×800, a run just weighed in the dark theme, the cup with a little ice in the dark theme, and a warning.
- **The calorimetry lab, after three pieces of its page were made shared with this one.** Its markup was written out before the change and after, at every step of a careful run of each part in both modes at two seeds, and was the same byte for byte. Its part 1 was then worked in the browser as far as the first entry of its worksheet, with the graph walked by pointer, by the arrow keys and by the buttons that step. The review worked that part to its end by the keyboard alone: the cup's steps, thirteen presses of Run on, which stood at one place, every one of twenty-seven readings walked with the arrow keys with its callout to a tenth of a degree, Home, End and Escape, the seven entries typed or chosen, and a run that counted, at 1.6% under the figure that lab holds; the Record tab showed it and the browser reported no error. The worksheet the two labs share was changed in one case by the review, for both: a list filled in Challenge and not checked, with the mode then changed to Guided, now has the button that checks it.
- **Names, and the contrast of the text and of the pictures (the review).** On three states of the page no button, field, choice box or summary was without a name; the graph is a picture with a name and a description, and the cup's drawing is hidden from assistive technology beside its words. The contrast of every piece of text on three states with every disclosure open was worked out from the colours the browser computed, in both themes: the least was 4.74 to 1 in the light theme, the grey of a secondary line at 12 and 14 px, and 6.94 to 1 in the dark; a figure to be read is 5.66 to 1, a tick 5.62 and the label of the entry that waits 5.0 in the light theme. The words of a button that cannot be used now are 3.71 to 1 in the light theme and 4.88 in the dark. On the graph the points, the two marks, the axes and their numbers are 19.8 to 1 in the light theme and 17.2 in the dark; the thin line through the points is 3.71 and 5.09; the grid's lines, which nothing is read from, are 1.36 and 1.50. In the cup the outlines are 19.8 and 17.2; the edge of a cube of ice against the liquid it floats in is 13.9 and 10.7; the liquid's own tint against the card is 1.43 and 1.61, and its surface is a line of 5.25 and 6.79. These are the figures of one browser's rendering of two themes, and are no claim of conformance.
- **By automated tests,** which run without a browser: the acceptance list of the design spec (section 23.6); the descriptions in words of the cup and of the graph; that the ice is drawn as six cubes, two and none; that over seeds 1 to 40 the fall on the graph is 30 units of its picture or more and a level stretch is level; that no reading is printed beside the graph; that the page of part 2 is the same, word for word and name for name, whichever of the three liquids the unknown is, at four steps up to the naming, at twelve seeds, in both modes; and that Challenge is shown nothing of what a result is held to before its notebook is checked.

**What was not checked:**

- No person has run the lab, by hand, by keyboard or by touch. No screen reader was used: that a warning, a refusal, the cup's state and a run's outcome are announced is known from the markup and from where focus went, and not from hearing them.
- One browser, headless Chromium on Linux. No real device, no real tablet, no real touch screen.
- The page with the text enlarged, at 200% zoom, in a high-contrast mode, or with the window between the sizes named.
- Whether a learner can tell a level stretch from a falling one on a real screen. The heights are measured in the picture; no person has looked.
- The hazard notes against the documents they rest on, in full. The review read every note as a learner sees it, and held the statements and the notes of the three sheets this lab read new, and of two of the government database's records, to the saved copies, where they agreed; the documents four of the notes share with other labs were not read again.
- No chemistry instructor has read the record, the notes or the primer.

**Known limits of the freezing point depression lab's page:**

- **A run's page is still more than two screens** of an upright tablet once its notebook is open: 2,246 to 2,714 px, the bench, the cup and the straining folded. The graph's card, 605 px, and the notebook are what is left.
- **At 1280×800 the cup's card and the graph's are more than one screen:** 932 px from the head of one to the foot of the other in part 2, where 735 px are under the tab bar. On an upright tablet they are inside one.
- **The graph's points and the second entry of the notebook are not in view together** on an upright tablet: from the top of the picture to the foot of that entry is 1,017 px. To the foot of the first entry it is 863 px, inside one screen. The callout of the reading looked at stays until another is looked at, and is at the picture's head.
- **The mark where the cup was strained stands close beside the last reading,** inside the ring drawn about that reading when it is looked at. It was looked at and left: the key names it, and it is where the logger stops.
- **Guided's warning of little ice is given once in a run** and is not stored: after a reload it is given once more.
- **A hundredth of a degree cannot be read from the graph's axis.** A reading is found by looking at its point: its callout says it, and so does a status line, for a screen reader.
- **A column of the graph is soon narrower than a finger:** 22 px with seventeen readings on an upright tablet, as in the calorimetry lab. The two buttons under the graph step from reading to reading.
- **The solute is not drawn.** That it is in the cup is said in the words beside the drawing, and by the tick on its step.
- **A reading typed in part 1 and held back by Guided's warning stays in its field,** with the warning's title under it; "Go on anyway" enters it.
- **What is typed and not yet entered is lost by a reload,** and so are the reading last looked at and a warning that was showing.
- Previous and Next are at the foot of a part, as in every lab that has the paged list.
- Below 768 pixels of width the Lab tab shows a notice in place of the bench, as in every lab.

### The types of reactions lab: accessibility

As above, there has been no audit and this is no claim of conformance.

**How it was checked, and by whom.** The lab's screens were built on 2026-10-12 and checked that day by the AI coding assistant that wrote them, by script, in headless Chromium on Linux, on the production build. Nobody sat at a keyboard. The page is built on the pieces of the labs before it: the frame and its tabs, the paged list, the form of choices, the hazards' tiles, the shared tube's drawing and the way a picture is explored, and the builder of an equation from tiles, which was the aqueous reactions lab's own and is now shared. What is new is the picture of a vessel. The lab's one deep review was held the same day by another AI coding assistant, which had built none of the lab: it worked by script in the same browser and by reading, made one change the project's owner had decided on (two stations were too long on an upright tablet), and put right what it found. What it ran is marked "(the review)" below; its record is the last section of the lab's research record (`docs/research/reactions/README.md`). No person took part in either.

**What was checked that way:**

- **Six whole labs in Guided, each from Start to the line that says the lab is complete.** Every look was made on the picture, every answer by its box, every equation tile by tile with its states and its numbers. Four by the pointer, at 768×1024 and at 1280×800 in the light and the dark theme; one by the keyboard alone, at 1280×800, each part of a picture looked at by moving focus to it and every button by Enter or Space; one by emulated touch, at 768×1024. The browser reported no error in any. In each, an equation with every number 1 was recorded first at the station where that does not balance, and was refused with a cross and the sentence that the atoms do not balance; the numbers were then raised and it was taken. A reload after the last station opened the Lab tab on the complete lab.
- **A whole lab in Challenge, by emulated touch (the review),** at 768×1024: at each station a run recorded wrongly and checked, then worked again right. An equation with an empty side was refused with its sentence; a species that does not belong, one missing, a state, the balance and the catalyst written on both sides were each taken without a mark, and each named when the station was checked; "No reaction" was said of a tube that reacts and a reaction, with an equation, of the tube that does not; the order was given wrong, and one metal given twice was refused; whole multiples of three equations counted. Counted in the live markup before each check: no tile and no place before a kind is said, then the station's six, seven or ten tiles and one empty place on each side whatever the equation is; no tick or cross but the action's own; no hint, no note, nothing over an arrow. A reload in the middle of a station kept what was recorded and not what was only chosen.
